### Writing decisions

| Choice | Why | From |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Jean's head after the chin turn has one position, written in the same words everywhere: 'his face turned toward the driver's seat, where she set it'. The phrase is used in V01-B07, B08, B09 and B11, SC01 and SC02. In SC18, at the motel table, the same position is his left, and she sits at his right. Before the reveal the phrase is 'head turned to the door' (V01-B01 to B06, V02-B01/B02, S01). The rule behind it: after death his head moves only when her hand moves it, stays where she sets it, and never lolls or bobs. In story order her hand first sets it in SC16, on the pillow. Correction: the previous version said B09, B11, SC02 and SC18 already matched, but they read 'still set toward the windshield'. That claim was wrong, and the text is now fixed. | In B08 she films from the driver's seat, so his open eyes sit frontal in her phone, and the make-or-break shot (A16) depends on that. In B09 she leans across his knees straight into his gaze. In SC18 his eyes are turned to his left while she sits on his right, so they aim past the lens because of where people sit, and the page doesn't have to say so. | Reviewer note (continuity that holds); A16; taste note (hands before faces) |
| Logged as deliberate stylization, not physiology: the dusk chin turn in V01-B07 comes about 18 hours after Jean dies in the night, when a real body would be rigid, and his face at 42 s is pale. Both stay as written. SC16 has her roll him onto his back in the morning, so the pilot no longer leaves him face-down all day against the pale face at 42 s. | True-crime followers will do this arithmetic. Naming it as a stylization, the same way the SC17 service bay is logged, tells every department it is a choice and not a mistake, and keeps anyone from fixing it with medical detail on screen. | Reviewer note (SC16); brief audience; A16 |
| SC16 now turns inside the scene. After his arm slides off her waist and 'J. Stay for breakfast.' gets no answer, she rolls him onto his back herself and turns his face toward her on the pillow, where it stays. She switches the speaker off, sits up and pulls the fitted corner off her side of the mattress: the first visible sign of the bed being stripped. SC17 opens with her pulling off the rest of the sheets. | Ref 06's 'waiting for him to turn over', as the brief describes it, is answered by her doing it for him. The scene ends on the turn's first visible consequence and not on a silence. | Craft brief ('Every scene turns, and the turn is visible'; 'end on the turn or its first visible consequence'); must-include (the morning after); reviewer note |
| SC09 keeps Lucas's dash-cam shot and makes the dash cam the one camera she keeps her face out of. At the curb JEAN books the ride on his own phone, and she types the drop-off: a corner a block from her building. She takes the seat directly behind the DRIVER, so the timestamped frame holds JEAN's face, her hands on his jaw and the back of her head, never her face. Flicking the dome light off darkens the lens as well as the driver's mirror. No in-world camera holds her face until the front camera in SC18. | It answers the true-crime viewer's first question, the ride record and the footage from the night he vanished, just as the SC17 bay answers the second (how he left the building). It also extends R2: she is never in his frame until the photograph. | Route R2 central idea; brief audience; reviewer note; the SC17 service-bay decision |
| The trance-soul track is source only, SC11-SC16. It plays from a small white speaker with a physical dial on her bedroom nightstand, beside her phone. SC11: heard through the bathroom door under the ice dropping into glasses. SC12: she starts it low while JEAN is in the bathroom, and her hands, squaring the two glasses, go still once it plays. SC15: her hand passes the phone and turns the dial up until his breathing is under the music. SC16: she switches it off. Every other scene carries its own sound. Bent on purpose: the taste note says music comes in late, if at all, but here it enters at about 14 minutes, mid-pilot. | The climax in SC15 needs the track planted a scene ahead. The brief says it plays to quiet her, and SC12 shows that in her hands. The dial answers how she plays it without touching her phone. The music stays inside the scene, and V01, V02 and the stills have none. | Craft brief (plant what the climax needs); tone (music); A08; verified fact (trance-soul music to quiet her); taste note (sound design before music: bent, flagged) |
| The meds appear only in SC17's clean-up, after the death. She wipes the cabinet shelf and lifts the four-week pill organizer: every compartment is still full, and a pharmacy fill slip taped inside its lid is dated five weeks before the dash cam's date (on screen, the date only). She wipes under it and squares it with the amber vial, whose label is turned to the wall. In SC15 her tactic is to wash her hands at the basin without meeting the mirror, and the cabinet stays shut. | A full organizer on its own reads as freshly filled. The dated slip makes 'untaken for weeks' visible on camera without naming a drug. Putting them after the death keeps them seen and never the reason. | Must-include (meds untaken for weeks); verified facts (meds in her bathroom; untaken for weeks); must avoid (meds as the explanation); reviewer note |
| The straw chain. The house lies flat on the counter as four walls and a pitched roof. Jean's own straw stands upright in the candle wax as the chimney (SC07). It is the only straw left at last call and goes into her pocket (SC08), stands in her ice (SC12) and is laid flat on the white table before the tin opens (SC13). In SC17 she drops the flat straw into the trash on top of the folded pink cloth and pockets only the tin. S03 now shows the chimney upright in the candle wax beside the flat house as her fingertip draws out a roof straw. | She isn't collecting trophies. The SC18 photograph is the only thing she keeps of him, against Jean's own wish in SC08 to take the chimney home as a trophy. The chimney is the detail that makes S03 belong to this show and not to any bar game. The tin opens only after his line (A18). | Route R2 central idea; brief S03; A04; A18; craft brief (an object that comes back meaning something else); reviewer note |
| D01 is written for execs. It opens with a FRONT page (logline; the proposition, pending Lucas; Venus in three sentences; Jean in two; tone in one). Every scene is present-tense story prose, with no 'X wants' labels. The wants, tactics, details and production notes live in the crew annex below. | The exec decides at D01, straight after V01, and should be reading a story, not a template. | Package review notes; strategy desired behavior |
| SC17 opens with a 4 s silent insert in the building's enclosed rear service bay: roller door down, the service lift's doors open, the two-seater beside them with the top down and the passenger seat empty, the only light from a high window. This is logged as a convenience of the world, like the stylization above. | True-crime followers will ask how she moved a dead man from a second-floor flat into an open car in daylight. A private bay beside a service lift answers that without showing the move. | Package review notes (world bible LOC-07); brief audience |
| SC04: she sweeps the prints and the incident report into the glovebox on top of the gun and snaps the side curtain into place before she crosses to the bar. The report's stamp reads NO CHARGES FILED. | Nothing incriminating is left on an open seat overnight, and the wording matches the US Southwest setting. | Package review notes (PR-09) |
| Two physical beats: in SC08's silent window montage he sets his fresh coupe down on the base straw, it rocks and he lifts it off, laughing; in SC11 he wets the tissue under the tap before he wipes. | The coupe re-wets the base straw's ring, so S03's wet ring is physically true. Dried drops on glazed china only come off pink with a damp tissue. | Package review notes (PR-32, PR-48); brief S03 |
| US vocabulary and spelling throughout (windshield, curb, trunk, tires, organizer, centered). The empty water jugs stand 'along the far wall' in SC03, V01-B12, B13 and S02. | Every department reads one vocabulary, and the jugs sit where the held frame can see them. | Package review notes (PR-23) |
| Crew annex, SC01-SC06. SC01 (V01 0-56 s): VENUS wants to film JEAN looking back at her. JEAN, dead, wants nothing, and his stillness is the obstacle. Her tactics: film him close, fix his collar at the stop sign, move her thumb to the flip icon. Detail: the stop sign nobody else will reach. From B07 on, his face stays turned toward the driver's seat, where she set it. SC02 (V01 56-72 s): VENUS wants proof nobody has found the motel. Detail: the unbroken dust on the office step. The pistol comes from the glovebox (the SC04 chain). SC03 (V01 72-90 s): VENUS wants the table set for two. The printer and the second chair plant SC18. SC04: VENUS wants to get in before he leaves alone again; the BARTENDER wants to close him out. Detail: the gun traded for the tin. The report and every case are fictional. The duffel plants V01-B11. SC05: VENUS wants him to agree to play; JEAN wants to finish his drink unseen; the BARTENDER wants the order in and the tab closed. Her tactics: the test question, the joke, the hand on his forearm, the mimosa dare; his is the stall. Detail: the first straw in the wet ring. The inciting incident lands at about 3:00. SC06: JEAN wants to be the wronged man in her eyes; VENUS wants the truth about his ex. His flaw under pressure: he lies to stay the wronged man. Detail: his denial of what nobody asked, with the packed duffel at his feet (A02, A04). | The crew keeps every want, tactic and note that stays out of the exec text. | Previous crew annex; reviewer notes |
| Crew annex, SC07-SC12. SC07: JEAN wants to take her home tonight; VENUS wants a reason to let him pass. Detail: the chimney straw in the wax. The white-sheets insert runs at real speed, with no nudity. SC08: JEAN wants a picture of the night, then the chimney as a trophy; VENUS wants him out the door with the verdict still hers; the BARTENDER wants the counter cleared. The roof straw pulled, with the chimney in the wax beside it, is the S03 frame. SC09: JEAN wants his hands inside her jacket; the DRIVER wants his back seat kept clean; VENUS wants the tin kept hers and her face out of the dash cam. The ride is on his phone to a corner, not a door; she sits directly behind the driver. The rideshare is unmarked (A10). Detail: the dash cam, the one camera she keeps her face out of. SC10: JEAN wants to know who she is and how she pays for this, but wants the night more; VENUS wants him inside without questions. Detail: his shoes and duffel on the white mat. SC11: JEAN wants to get back out to her fast. The drops belong to the previous man (A13) and are never explained on screen. The track is heard through the door. SC12: VENUS wants to quiet herself before he comes out, then the last question first; JEAN wants her in the bedroom now. The speaker, with a physical dial, sits on the bedroom nightstand beside her phone. The team shirt carries no logo (A10). Detail: her hands going still on the squared glasses; the straw standing in her ice. | The crew keeps every want, tactic and note that stays out of the exec text. | Previous crew annex; reviewer notes; must avoid (brands, explicit sex) |
| Crew annex, SC13-SC18. SC13: VENUS wants one true thing about love, his last straw; JEAN wants her in the bedroom. Detail: the wet mark on the white table. The tin's contents stay out of frame. SC14: JEAN wants her in the bed; VENUS wants him to keep walking. Detail: the drops at the threshold, where she never looks. SC15: VENUS wants to stop hearing his breathing. Her tactics: fold his clothes, wash her hands without meeting the mirror, turn the speaker's dial up. The breath catch is her climactic choice. No meds appear. SC16: VENUS wants him to turn over and reach for her. Her tactics: wait, lift his arm, say the line, then roll him over herself. Details: the four-bar loop; the fitted corner. The performance rule starts here, with his face on the pillow where her hand sets it. SC17: VENUS wants the apartment spotless before she takes JEAN away, with nothing of him kept. The meds: organizer full, fill slip in the lid dated five weeks before the dash cam's date, the vial's label to the wall, no drug name or diagnosis (PR-50, PR-51). The flat straw goes into the trash on top of the pink cloth; only the tin goes into her pocket. The sunglasses plant V01-B07. Detail: the pink cloth folded square. SC18: VENUS wants to get into one photograph with JEAN. She sits at his right, the side his face is turned away from, with the phone in front of them and a little to her right, so his eyes, turned to his left, miss the lens. Her fingers stop short of his chin. Detail: the printer's whir. The courthouse-steps prints show a fictional man. | The crew keeps every want, tactic and note that stays out of the exec text. | Previous crew annex; reviewer notes; must avoid (the tin's contents; meds as the explanation; real cases) |
| The whole piece is written for a 35mm film look. Her phone's upright frame keeps its compression, fingerprint smear and UI, and when she lowers the phone (V01-B06) the picture goes from phone video to film grain. The stills carry the same look. Stock, grain, gate, lenses and grade belong to the camera department. | Where Lucas's relayed request and the route differ, his request wins, and it sharpens the route's rule that sets her phone against the world as it is. | Relayed user request ('35mm film style'); route R2 visual language |
| The V01 REC clock runs without contradiction. The phone records without a break from 26.0 s to 52.0 s: B05 reads REC 01:04:29; B06's first 0.8 s (19 upright frames) shows REC 01:04:37, and the timer is hidden once the frame widens; from 52.0 s, B08 reads REC 01:04:55. The cut from B04 (00:53:10) to B05 is the only time jump. | With 26 s of recording between 26.0 s and 52.0 s, the timer lands on its own number inside one continuous take. | Package review (storyboard flag 3; camera plan SH09-SH16) |
| V02 runs on a teaser clock that is deliberately not frame-matched to V01: B01 reads 00:06:13, and B04-B06 read 01:04:55, 01:04:58 and 01:05:00. | The teaser has 15 s to carry an hour's drive and the reveal. It keeps V01's timer language without claiming V01's continuity. | Brief V02 ('rebuilt from V01 footage') |
| Jean's black duffel is planted: under his stool (SC04), packed at his feet during his lie (SC06), on his shoulder (SC08), at their feet (SC09), on the white mat (SC10), zipped by the door (SC17), and 'his black bag' in V01-B11. | V01-B11's bag has a source, and the packed bag contradicts his lie without any voiceover. | World bible blocker 1; taste note (restraint over explanation) |
| D01 ends with a one-page season engine: the season question, the weekly repeat (a new man, a new round of decorum, a new wall, the one photo afterwards) and the escalation (each house stands longer, the midseason truth-teller, the front camera on a living man, the tin in the finale). | The exec decides at D01, and the season question has to be on the page. | Brief D01 label (season question, A17); route R2 season extension |
| The pilot is a cold open, three acts and a tag across 18 scenes, about 30 minutes, framed by the FRONT and ENGINE pages. The inciting incident ('Have we met before?') lands at about 3:00. Act One ends with the last straw in her pocket, Act Two with the tin opening, and the tag with the first photo of them together. | Venus's own choices resolve the climax twice: she opens the tin after his line (SC13), and she turns the speaker's dial up over his catching breath (SC15), with no meds in view. | Craft brief (pilot); A01; A05; A06; A18; story seed |
| One continuous timeline: the bar (SC04) is Thursday night, SC05-SC17 run on into the next morning, the cold open is that evening, and the tag follows straight on. The pistol goes into the glovebox outside the bar (SC04) and comes out at the motel (SC02, V01 56-60 s). | The prints going back months show how long she has watched him, and the gun is accounted for from the bar to her back pocket. | Verified fact (gun in her back pocket at the motel); premise |
| V01 is SC01-SC03 in the order of the R2 execution example. The route's 56-64 s beat is split into the glovebox pistol (56-60 s) and the frontage check (60-64 s). B07 holds only the lift, the chin turn and the eyes. | The make-or-break eyes shot (A16) carries no extra hand action, and the room reads as the one she has kept. | Route R2 execution example; A14; A16 |
| The sunglasses carry continuity: she puts them on him in SC17, lifts them off in V01-B07, hooks them into her neckline in B08 and sets them by the oranges in SC18. | The object passes from him to her, and his face is bare for the tag photo. | Craft brief (plant an object that comes back) |
| V01 and V02 have no spoken lines, so every vo field is empty. Her laugh, written in the sound field, is the only voice. | In the white apartment the horror is how clean it is: dried drops only, on the toilet rim and at the threshold; the tin's contents out of frame; the finished room held longer than is comfortable. There is none in V01, V02 or the stills, and no slow motion anywhere. | This follows the clean tone and the must-avoid list, with ref 05's calm next to horror as the brief describes it. | Tone; must avoid (gore; the tin's contents; explicit sex; slow motion) |

Broken on purpose: S01 shows her laughing face, because a poster needs its lead; the V01-B04 and B08 fallbacks show part of her face for under a second, as the route accepts; the trance-soul track enters mid-pilot (see the music decision). 'The product is a tool, then a hero' doesn't apply, because there is no product. | The notes ask for a flag wherever one is broken. | Taste notes; route R2 notes |

## Cast

### VENUS

- **role in story**: Principal. A vigilante who watches unpunished men for months, then plays decorum with them over a date. She films everything and is never in her own frame. V01 objective: get JEAN to look back at her through her phone. Pilot want: one true thing about love from him, then one photograph with him. Obstacle: he is the man she is sentencing, he answers with lines, and by the cold open he is dead. Change: from never being in his frame to the first photo of them together (SC18), while the wall moves on to the next man. Age 30-38, target 34. Every face reads 30+ at the bar (alcohol, no market code given), and she is old enough to have kept a wall for months and paid for the white apartment. Defaults: I keep Lucas's height, slimness and long brown hair with highlights. I break the genre default (a glossy femme fatale in her late 20s) and the generator's stock face (symmetrical, poreless, smiling at the lens) with pores, laugh lines, one higher brow and marked working hands. She smiles at him, never at us, until SC18. Heritage: Black American, recorded here and never put in a prompt. It is a casting choice, not a story need, and Lucas approves it (see blockers).
- **screen presence**: In one frame (in the left-hand-drive car the script now places): a woman in her mid-thirties, left hand on the wheel, right hand holding a phone out toward the man on her right. Her shoulders are loose and her eyes are on him, not the road. The read: in love, not driving. Her hands lead (taste note: hands before faces). At the stop sign her left hand crosses to his collar, brushes the dust off and straightens it. Her right thumb rests on the flip icon, then slides away. At the motel she leans across his knees into his open gaze and doesn't look away. She handles things slowly, and whatever she puts down lands square: cans, his shoes on the white mat, the two glasses, his folded clothes, the pink cloth. Between actions she is still and watching. She laughs with her whole torso, so the phone shakes. At the bar she sits turned fully to him, elbows off the counter, one fingertip on the straws. In the rideshare she keeps the back of her head to the dash cam, even while laughing against his mouth. In SC14 she never looks down at the drops. In SC17 she finds them herself.
- **performance style**: Naturalistic, played as a love story. The laugh is the only heightened thing: real, from the belly, never a cackle. One laugh voice is locked across V01, V02 and D01: low, breaking high at the end, with no words. It syncs to her face only at 34-42 s and in the front-camera fallbacks, and it softens after the eyes (V01-B08). At rest her face stays pleasant and busy with him: no knowing glance, no half-closed stare, no lip bite (taste note: restraint over explanation). At the bar she blinks less than he does. Beside the dead JEAN she blinks normally, so in a shared frame her blink is the one difference between them. Lens rule: her eyes never go into any lens, including the under-a-second flips in the V01-B04 and B08 fallbacks, where they stay on him. The first time is SC18, into her own front camera. D01 is sync dialogue, dry and quick. She plays the flirt as genuine because she hopes he will pass (A18), and her verdicts land with no change of face ('That's a drink'). Repeated gesture: a digit rests, then slides off. Her right thumb does it on the flip icon, and her fingertip does it on the chimney straw (SC07) and on the tin's lid until his line (SC12-SC13). SC15: she washes her hands without meeting the mirror. When his breath stops and rasps, her hand passes the phone without resting on it and turns the dial up at the pace she fixed his collar, with no change of face. SC16 opens as the one stretch where her hands have nothing to do: she lies still, a hand to her forehead, eyes on the back of his head. 'J. Stay for breakfast.' comes at the pitch of 'That's a drink', not pleading. Then she rolls him over, turns his face on the pillow, switches off the speaker and pulls the fitted corner, all with the care she gave his collar. SC17: she lifts the pill organizer with the same face and hands as the padlock check (V01-B10), wipes under it and sets it back square. There is no wince and no pause on the full compartments or the fill slip, so the meds are seen and never played as the reason. The missed drops get one beat of stillness, then she kneels and scrubs. SC18 is the first time her thumb presses.
- **wardrobe fit notes**: 177 cm: 7 cm shorter than JEAN, 3 cm shorter than DRIVER, 7 cm taller than BARTENDER. Long neck and arms, lean, with visible forearm muscle (she rolls and dresses JEAN in SC16-SC17); shoulders narrower than hips. Right-handed: the phone (PR-01) is in her right hand and the pistol rides in her right back pocket. Her left hand does the collar. US women's 4 / S, tall sleeve length. Constraints: both sleeves stop above the wrist bone and nothing covers the backs of her hands, so the right-hand scar, the right-thumb nick and the left-index burn stay clear. No ring on the right thumb or the left index finger. While she walks (V01-B10), the right back pocket shows a compact pistol grip above the pocket line without dragging the waistband. The V01 neckline holds one open arm of the sunglasses (V01-B08 to SC18). The bar jacket's pocket takes the tin and a straw with no bulge and can be reached from inside the jacket (SC08-SC09). The SC12 T-shirt covers to mid-thigh and carries no logo (A10). No nudity in any piece. In the open car her hair holds one way for all of V01 (loose or tied, the stylist's call) and never falls over the hand shots.
- **visual identity anchors**: [macro] Build: 177 cm, long neck and long arms, lean forearms, narrow shoulders over wider hips.; [macro] Natural hair dark brown #3A2417: loose 3B curls, dense, soft rounded hairline. Worn past the shoulder blades, with grown-out honey highlights #B98A55 from mid-length (joint lock, stylist). The only long hair in the cast.; [micro] Long face, narrow jaw, heavy straight brows with the right one 3 mm higher; eyes dark brown #4A2E1C.; [micro] Skin #A36A48 at the cheekbone: open pores across the nose and inner cheeks, fine laugh lines at the outer eyes, three small dark freckles on the left cheekbone.; [micro] Hands (joint lock, hand double; locked by the director's ruling): long fingers, prominent knuckles, three pale #C99D80 marks. Right hand: a 2 cm scar across the back between the thumb and index knuckles, and a 6 mm nick across the back of the thumb at its first joint. Left hand: a 1 cm glossy oval burn on the back of the index finger, just above the knuckle. No other marks.
- **fictional talent**: true

### JEAN

- **role in story**: Principal. St. Jean, called J: a serial dater three nights into drinking over a breakup. His ex's domestic-violence report ended with no charges filed (A02; the case is fictional). Bar objective: win the game and take her home. Apartment objective: get her to the bedroom. Want: to stay the wronged man in her eyes and win the night. Obstacle: every lie costs him a straw, and she already knows the answers. Change: from alive to dead, and from trying to frame the two of them himself (SC08) to being framed with her once he can no longer look (SC18). In V01 his job is to be carried: dead weight, with the one face shot at 42 s. Age 34-42, target 38. Lines and a thickening waist show the charm is older than the looks it was built on. This breaks the category default (a sculpted late-20s leading man) and the generator's drift toward young and symmetrical, so his 'hit list' swagger reads as habit. Heritage: Haitian-American, recorded here and never put in a prompt. The reason is the name St. Jean and the family D01 can give him. He is Black like VENUS so the killing doesn't read as a cross-racial story the pilot isn't telling. Lucas confirms this with S01 and V02 in view (see blockers).
- **screen presence**: In one frame (V01): a big man in black in the passenger seat, cheek on the headrest, head turned to the door and away from her camera, so only his left jaw, ear, neck and collar show. Nothing moves but his hair, and he could be asleep. After her fingers turn his head (V01-B07), his face stays turned toward the driver's seat, where she set it, through the motel and at the table. Alive, in SC04 he sits with his shoulders rounded over the glass, elbow planted, packed duffel under the stool: a man drinking to be left alone. When she speaks he props himself up, turns his stool to her, pulls the candle over and keeps his eyes on hers. His hands join her game: a straw stood upright in the wax for himself, his phone held out to fit them both, a reach for the chimney as a trophy. In the rideshare his hands go inside her jacket for the pocket. From the lobby on, his eyes go to price and surfaces (the marble, the white room) before they go to her. He checks his teeth before he deals with the blood. At the threshold he stops half a step at the drops, then steps over them.
- **performance style**: Three registers. At the bar (SC04-SC09) he is naturalistic and really lifted: slack and heavy in SC04, then up from her first line. He laughs at her lines, never at his own, and keeps his eyes on hers across the candle. Pushing the tab slip back unsigned is the last time he looks at it. He plays the flirt for real, so the audience wants the house to stand (Lucas: 'Jean spirits began to uplift'). The SC06 lie ('She left. Packed while I was at work. I never once raised my voice.') comes out easy, with no tell, because he believes it. SC09 is appetite, clothes on, laughing into her. From the lobby (SC10-SC14) the habit shows. His eyes reach the room before they reach her, and his lines fall into a rehearsed cadence ('Who said Rome was built in a day?', 'I ain't won a ring yet'). It peaks in the SC13 speech: a man stretching for depth and missing, who covers the stumble with 'That's for you to find out.' Sync dialogue throughout. SC15 is breathing only, recorded wild, face down with no lip-sync: slow and loud, one long stop, then a rasp. Dead (V01, SC16-SC18, S01): absolute stillness. No blink, no swallow, no breath in the chest. His eyes are open the same amount in every shot, upper lid at the top edge of the iris, aimed past the lens and never into it. Body rule (the treatment's performance rule, which the script logs as stylization, not physiology): his head moves only when her hand moves it, stays where she leaves it, and never lolls or bobs, whether the car is moving or he is at rest. SC16: his arm is loose and slides off her waist. She rolls him onto his back and turns his face toward her on the pillow, and it stays there. SC17: his arms still bend as she dresses him. V01-B01 to B06: his head holds, turned to the door, through every jolt and jump cut. V01-B07: her two fingers turn his head alone on the neck, about 60°, from the door toward the driver's seat. His torso and shoulders stay square in the seat, the collar moves only where the jaw drags it, and the head stops where she leaves it. SC18: her fingers lift toward his chin and stop, so the head stays turned to his left and his eyes miss her lens. If the director restores a push there, the push does not move it. Silent in V01 and V02.
- **wardrobe fit notes**: 184 cm: 7 cm taller than VENUS, 4 cm taller than DRIVER, 14 cm taller than BARTENDER. Broad shoulders well wider than the hips, thick neck, waist thickening over the belt. He must plausibly lift and carry VENUS in SC14 and read as real weight when she rolls and dresses him (SC16-SC17). Right-handed: he stands the straw and holds the phone out with his right hand (SC07-SC08). US men's L, 36 waist. Constraints: one black outfit carries SC04-SC18, because she dresses him again in SC17. The black reads as one mass against white rooms and gold light, yet lets dust glint at the collar (V01-B05). With his cheek on the headrest, the collar stands clear of the jaw, and nothing covers the side of the neck facing the driver, so collar, jaw and mole read as separate shapes from 0 to 42 s. The neckline is loose enough that a 60° head turn on a square torso drags the collar only at the jaw line and never pulls the shoulders (V01-B07). Nothing rides up or gapes on dead weight. His face takes sunglasses, with the arm on the driver's side visible. His seated head clears a small 1950s two-seater's windshield (A11; production design confirms).
- **visual identity anchors**: [macro] Build: 184 cm, broad shoulders well wider than the hips, thick neck, waist thickening over the belt.; [macro] Natural hair black #17110E: tight 4A curls, dense; squared hairline, with the temple on the side facing the driver (his left) receding further. Top at 12-15 cm stretched length, picked out at the crown so it moves as one mass in the wind; sides tapered short; close beard with a hard cheek line (joint lock, stylist).; [micro] Short, wide face, wide square jaw, heavy low brows that almost meet; irises dark brown #2A1A12, unchanged in death. Dead eyes (V01-B07, B08, V02-B04 to B06, SC16-SC18): upper lid at the top edge of the iris in every shot; cornea dry and dulled with a faint grey film and no wet shine; dust on the lashes from the drive on. 'Pale, lost' lives in these details, never in a lighter iris (joint lock, makeup/VFX).; [micro] Skin #6E4631 at the cheekbone: visible pores on the nose and cheeks, two faint forehead lines, crow's feet. Dead state (V01, SC16-SC18, S01): the same hex desaturated, grey-ash at the lips, lids and under the eyes, dust in the creases after the drive. Never lightened by makeup, the grade or the 35mm look (joint lock, makeup).; [micro] A dark, lentil-sized mole on the left side of the neck (the side facing the driver), below the ear and above the collar. It is the one mark visible in the turned-head frames (0-42 s), and no other character has it.
- **fictional talent**: true

### BARTENDER

- **role in story**: Supporting, one line. Works the dive bar's Thursday late shift. Objective: close JEAN's tab and clear the counter before last call. What only he does: his tab slip sends VENUS in (SC04), and the order goes through him. Age 46-54, target 50: well past 30 for the alcohol rule, and twelve years older than JEAN. Both onlookers are men because the script calls each 'him' or 'he'. Heritage: Mexican-American, recorded here and never put in a prompt. It is a casting choice, not a story need: the two onlookers are cast apart from each other and from the leads, so no heritage is tied to one job.
- **screen presence**: In one frame: a thick-set man of about fifty behind the bar, forearms on the counter, eyes on the tab. He wants to close up. He sets the slip face-down in front of JEAN without looking at it or at him, pours without watching, and reaches for glasses the moment they are empty. Nothing he puts down lands straight. He looks at the couple only when an order is due.
- **performance style**: Understated, deadpan. At rest his eyes are on the tab, the glasses and the till. He has one sync line ('What can I get you guys?'), said half to the counter, and no reaction to the flirt. When JEAN slides the slip back unsigned, he takes it without a look. At last call he clears the glasses without a word. In SC04 and the SC08 window shots he is background only.
- **wardrobe fit notes**: 170 cm: 14 cm shorter than JEAN, 10 cm shorter than DRIVER, 7 cm shorter than VENUS. Barrel chest, shoulders and hips the same width, heavy forearms. Right-handed. US men's XL. Constraints: forearms bare to the elbow, so the hand that sets down the slip reads. No logo on him or behind him (A10).
- **visual identity anchors**: [macro] Build: 170 cm, barrel chest, shoulders and hips the same width, heavy bare forearms.; [macro] Shaved head with grey stubble #8E8882 at the sides and back; full grey-black moustache (joint lock, stylist). The only shaved head in the cast.; [micro] Round face, wide jaw softening under the chin, heavy greying brows with a pale scar splitting the outer end of the left brow; eyes dark brown #3A2618.; [micro] Skin #B07B55: deep forehead lines, crow's feet, broken capillaries across the nose.
- **fictional talent**: true

### DRIVER

- **role in story**: Featured, silent. A rideshare driver on a night shift, unbranded (A10), on a ride JEAN booked. Objective: keep his back seat clean; the dome light is his only move. What only he does: he watches them without being part of her game, and his dash cam is the one camera in the pilot she keeps her face out of. Age 58-64, target 61 (under 70, so no flag). His silver crown makes him the oldest face, and it reads at dash-cam resolution. Heritage: white American, recorded here and never put in a prompt. It is a casting choice, for the same reason as BARTENDER's.
- **screen presence**: In one frame, from the cabin-facing dash cam: a thin, grey-haired man sitting upright at the edge of the frame, both hands on the wheel, his outline against the side window as streetlight slides past, with VENUS in the seat directly behind him. His right hand rises to the dome switch and flicks it on with one finger; he doesn't turn his head. His eyes go to the mirror. When VENUS switches the light off he leaves it off, and his eyes keep lifting to the mirror.
- **performance style**: Understated, silent. He judges with his eyes only: they lift to the mirror, hold for about two seconds, then go back to the road. No reaction face and no words. The dash-cam frame is his whole performance space.
- **wardrobe fit notes**: About 180 cm; he reads long in the seat, head a hand's width below the roof lining. He is 4 cm shorter than JEAN and 3 cm taller than VENUS, but never stands beside either. Narrow, sloping shoulders; thin arms. Right-handed: his right hand goes to the dome switch. US men's M. Constraints: his head and shoulders must read as a clear silhouette against the side window and the passing streetlight at dash-cam resolution. His right sleeve stays clear of the wrist, so the hand reads when it rises into the dome light. Nothing branded on him or the car (A10).
- **visual identity anchors**: [macro] Seated high and thin: about 180 cm, narrow sloping shoulders, long neck, a profile that reads against the side window.; [macro] Thinning silver hair #CFC9C0 cropped close, receding high, the crown bright under the dome light; clean-shaven (joint lock, stylist). The only silver hair in the cast.; [macro] Right hand shape: long, bony fingers and a narrow wrist, the outline that rises into the dome light to the switch.; [micro] Backs of the hands: raised veins, swollen knuckles, brown age spots on pale skin.; [micro] Long, narrow face, narrow jaw, heavy grey brows; skin #DDBBA3 with red weathering across the cheeks and nose, and a brown age spot at the left temple.
- **fictional talent**: true

Size: at least 3% of V01's 16:9 frame width (58 px or more at 1920, about 9.5% of the pillar's width) and at least 6% of V02's 9:16 frame width (65 px or more at 1080). It is in the A10 clearance package. Contrast: REC dot on the bar 3.8:1; timer and icon on the bar 16.3:1; Jean's #24262B collar on the #D9CBB0 headrest 9.4:1. Clear zone: the lower-right 15% of the frame, around the icon, holds nothing but her thumb (V01-B04 and B08, V02-B06). Swap-test stand-in: the icon exists only because she won't turn the lens on herself, and its first press is SC18.

### LOC-02

- **description**: SC01 (V01-B01 to B08, V02-B01 to B06, and the light for S01). US Southwest high desert, 2026 ASSUMED, within a day's drive of the city. A crossroads town empty for decades ASSUMED: one block of six single-story stucco storefronts, windows boarded and sign bands sanded blank. A two-lane blacktop runs north past it through scrub flats toward low hills. Right-hand traffic, a faded double-yellow center line, white edge lines. No other vehicle, person, animal or overhead wire in any shot; only the scrub moves. She passes the town twice, so the storefronts in V01-B03 at 00:21:40 are a second pass. Wear: crazed asphalt; the stop sign's face sun-chalked from red to #B8453A, letters intact, and the sign spun on its rusted post by years of wind to face the crossing instead of its approach, with nobody come to set it straight (ASSUMED, PR-10); a sand drift across the northbound lane at the crossing that no tire crosses until hers. One real detail: the stop sign nobody else will ever reach (V01-B02).
- **spatial layout**: Exterior. x runs north along the blacktop from the town block's south end and y east from the center line, in m. Road 7.3 m wide (two 3.65 m lanes); the car keeps to the northbound lane, its passenger side at about y 2.6, Jean's head at about y 2.05. Town block on the east side (Jean's side) at x 0-45, set back 4 m from the edge line, sign bands 4.2 m high. Crossroads with an east-west graded gravel road, 6 m wide (edges x 3,097-3,103), 3.1 km north of the town: 6 min 08 s at about 30 km/h (19 mph) from the first pass at REC 00:00:04 to the stop at 00:06:12. No painted stop line (worn away). PR-10 stands on the east shoulder 2.2 m off the edge line (y 5.85), set back 9 m from the gravel edge at x 3,088, where the shoulder widens over a culvert ASSUMED. B02: she stops with her front bumper 1 m short of the gravel edge (x 3,096), PR-58 just ahead of it. Jean's head, 2.9 m behind the bumper, is then about 5.1 m north and 3.8 m west of the sign, so from the phone over the dash the sign sits about 7° right of and 11° above his head, facing the lens, with the empty crossing out of frame ahead. Loop ASSUMED, 15 min 28 s from 00:06:12 to the second pass at 00:21:40: east 2.0 km on the gravel, south 4.6 km on a gravel section road, west 2.0 km back to the blacktop 1.5 km south of the town (8.6 km at about 40 km/h, 25 mph: 12.9 min), then north 1.5 km on the blacktop at about 35 km/h, 22 mph (2.6 min). Second pass (B03, 00:21:40): north through the town again, past the crossroads (never framed), into open desert (00:38:15, about 11 km on) and toward hills 2-5 km east (00:52:30). The low sun sits in the west, behind the driver; blue dusk by B05.
- **palette**: #9A8466 dominant: caliche ground and dry scrub, matte, 36%; #D8B77A secondary: low-sun sky and haze, V01-B01 to B04, 24%; #3E5670 secondary: blue dusk sky, replacing it from V01-B05, 24%; #4A4744 secondary: crazed asphalt, matte, 18%; #C8B79A neutral: bleached stucco storefronts, flat, 12%; #B8453A accent: sun-chalked stop-sign sheeting, 1%
- **product placement**: No product. The stop sign carries the scene's one detail, and 'STOP' is the only word here. The only motion is the scrub, which stays lower in contrast than Jean's #24262B collar in the same frame.

Pocket map from V01-B09: phone outline in the right front pocket, keys in the left front, grip in the right back. Only the grip shows.

### LOC-04

- **description**: The motel room: SC03 (V01-B11 to B14, S02) and SC18. Room 3 of the 1958 block ASSUMED, stripped (no bed frame, no TV). Venus has used it on and off for about five weeks ASSUMED, counted by nine emptied gallon jugs. It is where she builds the wall and brings each man afterwards (A14). Walls: period knotty-pine tongue-and-groove in 15 cm vertical boards, aged to amber #7A5A3C under dull satin varnish; pins go straight in. Two-slot NEMA 1-15 outlets with brown bakelite plates, all dead; the wall-B plate at y 120, 30 cm up, is missing. Before V01-B12 she left at least two days ago ASSUMED, with the bedroll laid out, the wall up and the lantern off on the table. Wear, all inside the held frame: a chalky dust film #A08D72 over the fir floorboards, broken where her feet go at y 380-480. There, a bare track 40 cm wide, dull satin #5C4A38, runs along y 440-480 behind the chairs from the bedroll head (x 80) to the jugs (x 330), and a 50 x 40 cm scuffed patch in the same #5C4A38 lies at x 125-175, y 380-420, under the table's north edge where her feet go when she sits. Three overlapping jug rings, 16 cm across, in dull #9C8468 on the table's west end at x 122-150, y 372-398. A paler 60 x 80 cm rectangle #A88458 on wall C at x 283-343, heights 155-235, where the motel's own picture hung. About 20 empty pin holes, 1 mm with 3 mm dark halos #4A3626, at x 278-284, where she re-pinned the last column. Background: nothing moves; the wind pushes at the door. One real detail: PR-25, the printer, planted for SC18.
- **spatial layout**: 3.6 W x 4.8 D x 2.6 H ASSUMED. Walls clockwise: A south (entrance), B west, C north, D east. Anchor A/B; x along A toward D and y toward C, in cm; bearing 0° = toward C (compass north). Wall A: window at x 40-140 (sill 95, head 200), boarded with plywood #8C7A5E, a 15 cm gap at the top showing dark sky. Door PR-15 at x 250-340, hinged at x 340, swings in toward D over x 250-340, y 0-90; it sticks at 35° until shouldered. View out: the carport and the car. PR-05 and PR-06 lie just inside the latch-side jamb at x 190-245, y 10-50, below the held frame; in SC18 she takes PR-28 out of PR-06 there and drops PR-02 in. Wall D: PR-19 S1 at x 315-360, y 280-325, facing 270°. North-east corner: the nine emptied jugs PR-23 at x 330-360, y 400-475. Wall B: bedroll PR-26 at x 10-80, y 190-400, with the wall-B section of PR-20 above it at y 205-380, heights 150-234. Wall C: the main PR-20 at x 15-277.5, heights 95-239; the bare pine at x 280-345, with the paler rectangle, stays empty until SC18. Table PR-17 (120 x 70 x 74) at x 120-240, y 330-400. Chairs PR-18 and PR-19 stand on its north side with their backs to wall C; the back tops at 88 cm sit below the photos' 95 cm bottom edge. They face 180°, seat centers at y 420, x 150 (hers) and x 210 (Jean's). Tabletop before B12: PR-16 (off) at x 180, y 365, and the rings. From B12: PR-16; PR-25 at x 145, y 340, slot facing 180°; PR-24 at x 195-225, y 340-360; PR-21 at x 182-198, y 382-397; PR-22 at x 206-238, y 382-397; PR-07 folded flat beneath. SC18 adds only PR-04 at x 200-214, y 365-370. SC18 seating: Jean in PR-19 facing 180°, torso square, head set 30° to his left at about 150° (the car's P2), his gaze reaching wall D at about y 160; Venus in PR-18 at x 165, on his right (west), the side his face is turned from. Dressing master (V01-B13, S02 and SC18's end; placement is the DP's): read from the door end facing wall C. The floor enters frame only beyond about y 375, which is why the jugs and the wear sit at y 380-480.
- **palette**: #7A5A3C dominant: knotty-pine paneling, aged amber, dull satin varnish, 30% of the S02 master; #A08D72 secondary: dust film over the fir floorboards, chalky flat, 14%; #1E2833 secondary: night tones in about half the prints, gloss photo paper, 14%; #A09A90 secondary: daylight concrete and asphalt in the other half, gloss photo paper, 11%; #5E4632 neutral: oiled pine table and chairs, worn satin, 12%; #24262B accent: Jean's black clothes, the figure in every print, 4%
- **product placement**: No product. The jobs here belong to PR-20 (none of them together) and to PR-25 with PR-27, the pilot's payoff (A06). The printer sits on Venus's side because she works it; the oranges and, in SC18, the sunglasses sit in front of Jean. The lantern #F2EEE6 on the #5E4632 table gives 7.6:1. No gun on the table in SC18: PR-02 goes into PR-06 at the jamb, below the held frame. Frame lock: V01-B13 and S02 name more than five props, over the craft limit but forced by the script. Lock order: PR-20, PR-16, PR-24, PR-25, PR-19; PR-21, PR-22, PR-23 and PR-26 are held by count and position. Nothing moves in V01-B13 to B14.

### LOC-05

- **description**: The dive bar and its street: SC04 (seen from the car), SC05 to SC08, and S03. A dive bar in an unnamed US Southwest city, 2026, open about 40 years ASSUMED, in a 1950s single-story strip of painted brick #7E4B3A with a flat stucco parapet and one fan palm in a sidewalk cutout. LED cobra-head streetlights, so the only amber on the sidewalk is the bar's window spill. Inside is a long, narrow regulars' room, after refs 01-03 as the brief describes them: amber wall sconces, a red neon strip over the back bar, a green-glowing bottle fridge, candles in glasses on the counter, and a chalkboard menu smudged past reading. Thursday, near empty: three patrons in silhouette at the far end and one bartender. It is Jean's third night on the same stool: in PR-56 with PR-04 hooked in his placket, PR-41 on the footrail and PR-05 under the stool, all first seen here through the window in SC04. Wear: the varnish worn to raw #6B4A30 over a 40 x 25 cm patch at his stool from elbows; dozens of faint dull rings #5A4030 on the counter; the brass footrail rubbed bright #C9A15A in front of each stool. Background: the patrons move slowly, stay lower in contrast than the straws, and never cross the straw house or the candle. One real detail: PR-30 standing in candle wax.
- **spatial layout**: 6.0 W x 14.0 D x 3.2 H ASSUMED. Walls: A street front (south), B west, C back, D east. Anchor A/B; x along A toward D and y toward C, in cm. Wall A: glazed door at x 470-560, swinging in toward D; a plain front window with no lettering at x 40-440, sill 80, head 270. View out: the street and, 14 m away at the far curb, the car (LOC-01), driver's side to the bar. Wall B: back-bar shelves at y 150-1250, bartender's aisle at x 45-100, counter at x 100-160, top 107 cm, running y 200-1200. Ten stools at 90 cm centers from y 260, seats at 76 cm. Jean is on stool 2 (y 350), in the window's sightline, with PR-05 at x 175-225, y 336-364; Venus takes stool 3 (y 440). PR-31 starts at x 115, y 260; the straw caddy stands at x 130, y 480; the straw house is centered at x 130, y 395, between their glasses. Red neon at 210 cm over y 250-900; fridge at y 950-1050. Wall C: restroom door at x 400-480. Wall D: booths, sconces at 190 cm. Street: right-hand traffic, 12 m curb to curb, 3 m sidewalks, LED cobra heads (4000K ASSUMED), one fan palm at x 620. The rideshare (LOC-06) stops at the bar's curb, x 0-500, right side to the curb.
- **palette**: #1A120D dominant: brown-black shadow on walls and ceiling, flat, 42%; #3A2418 secondary: varnished counter, satin, 16%; #C8873A secondary: amber sconce and candle glow on brass and glass, 10%; #9E1B1B accent: red neon strip, 3%; #2F6B4F accent: green bottle-fridge glow, 3%; #EFE9DC accent: white paper straws, uncoated matte, 1%
- **product placement**: No product. Decorum is read in straws, so the straws do the job here. White #EFE9DC on the #3A2418 counter gives 12:1, so the house reads with the sound off in S03 and through the window in SC08. In S03 nothing but her fingertip moves within 30 cm of the house, and the chimney (PR-30 S1) may stand at the frame edge. Must stay blank: every bottle label on the back bar (blank cream #D8CFBC paper), the fridge glass and its contents, the chalkboard (smudges only), the neon (a plain line), the coasters, the window, PR-04's arms and PR-05.

### LOC-06

- **description**: SC09: the bar curb, then the rideshare's back seat, seen from a cabin-facing dash cam. A generic late-model midsize sedan, dark gray #2F3236, with no badges and no rideshare stickers, logos or trade dress (A10). The driver keeps a clean cabin: the rear seat cloth is pilled to #3A3D42 fuzz over 25 x 15 cm at both outer bolsters, and a plain black phone mount sits on the dash. Jean's duffel lies across the footwell. Background: cool LED streetlight slides across the side glass; nothing outside is legible. One real detail: the dash cam, a second recording of Jean that Venus doesn't control and keeps her face out of.
- **spatial layout**: Cabin 1.50 W x 2.00 L x 1.15 H ASSUMED. Walls: A the right (curb) side, with the right rear door at x 40-110 as the entry; B the rear shelf and back glass; C the left side, doors shut; D the windshield and dash. Anchor A/B, the right rear corner; x along A toward D (forward) and y toward C, in cm; bearing 0° = toward C, clockwise (90° D, 180° A, 270° B). Rear bench at x 20-70, y 5-145, seat 35 cm. Venus sits at y 100, directly behind the driver; Jean sits at y 40 by the door, slouched into the right rear corner. PR-01 is in her right chest pocket, and PR-03 and PR-30 in her inside left pocket, which his hand reaches inside the jacket. PR-05 lies across the footwell at x 72-100, y 45-95. Driver front left at y 95-135 (US left-hand drive). Dash cam PR-36 at the top of the windshield at x 172, y 110, on the driver's side of the rearview mirror (y 75), lens facing 270° onto the bench. From there Jean in the far corner faces it, and Venus, leaning over him, shows it the back of her head; the blocking is the director's. Dome light PR-37 in the headliner at x 55, y 75, 110 cm above the floor, within her reach.
- **palette**: #0E0F12 dominant: unlit cabin, headliner and pillars, 48%; #2C2F33 secondary: charcoal cloth rear bench, matte, 20%; #24262B secondary: Jean's black shirt, with the #141414 duffel, 12%; #B9C3CC accent: cool LED streetlight sliding across the glass and faces, 4000K ASSUMED, 5%; #E9E4D8 accent: lit dome-light lens, one beat only, 2%
- **product placement**: No product. PR-03 is present but never framed. Must stay blank: the car's badges and any windshield stickers, the dash-cam body, the phone mount, the driver's phone screen (off), PR-35's screen and PR-05.

### LOC-07

- **description**: Venus's building, the lobby and the stairs to her door: SC10. A renovated 1920s downtown mid-rise in the same unnamed US Southwest city, 2026 ASSUMED, where she has lived about two years ASSUMED. At night there is no doorman, and no residents are seen or heard. Honed white marble, satin brass and a quiet stair. Wear: the marble is polished to a gloss path 90 cm wide from the street door to the stair foot (#EDEAE4 gloss within #E9E6E0 honed). One real detail: the marble curtail step at the stair foot, rounded and projecting, the one she warns him about ('Mind the step'). The enclosed rear service bay behind the lobby is LOC-11.
- **spatial layout**: Lobby 8.0 W x 6.0 D x 4.2 H, floor to floor 4.40 m ASSUMED. Walls: A street (south), B west, C north, D east. Anchor A/B; x along A toward D and y toward C, in cm; bearing 0° = toward C. Wall A: a glazed brass-framed double street door at x 300-480, swinging in. Wall B: mailbox bank PR-38, 120 x 90 cm, at y 150-270, its bottom 100 cm up. A marble U-stair in the north-east corner: 120 cm flights, 25 risers of 17.6 cm and 28 cm treads. Flight 1 (13 risers) rises north along wall D at x 680-800, y 120-456; its first riser is the curtail step, projecting 20 cm to x 660. The half landing is at x 560-800, y 456-600, 229 cm up. Flight 2 (12 risers) rises south at x 560-680 through an open well to the second-floor landing at x 560-800, y 20-148. Her door PR-39 is on the landing's east side, facing 270°, so she walks east into LOC-08. A satin brass handrail runs on the wall side at 90 cm. Outlets: US NEMA 5-15.
- **palette**: #E9E6E0 dominant: honed white marble floor and treads, 38%; #F4F2EE secondary: white eggshell stair-hall walls, 30%; #B08D57 secondary: satin brass handrail, mailboxes and door frames, 8%; #9A9A98 neutral: gray marble veining, 6%; #24262B accent: Jean in black, 5%
- **product placement**: No product. Must stay blank: the mailbox cards, the door and any wall directory. The marble is the only thing here that reads as money, and nothing names what she does.

### LOC-08

- **description**: The white apartment's entry, living room and hall to the bedroom: SC10 (from the door), SC11 (off-screen sets), SC12 to SC14 and SC17. Her lure apartment (A14), on the second floor, entered walking east from the LOC-07 landing. White on white, and nothing sits on a surface unless a beat needs it. Kept about two years ASSUMED and cleaned daily. Before SC10 she vacuumed the rug, leaving fresh stripes, and filled the ice bucket. She missed the three drops at the bedroom threshold, which belong to the previous man (A13). Off screen in SC11 she hangs her jacket on PR-62, sets PR-03 on the table, takes PR-01 to the bedroom nightstand and changes into an oversized team T-shirt. In SC12, on screen, she takes PR-30 from the jacket into her glass. Wear, all of it marks of cleaning: 30 cm vacuum stripes on the rug, alternating matte and sheen. After SC17 the scrubbed 30 x 30 cm patch at the threshold is flatter and whiter (#F4F3EF matte) than the satin floor (#EFEDE8). A taste note is broken on purpose: there is no dish rack and no charging cable, because the tidiness is the horror (the clean tone; ref 05 as the brief describes it). One real detail: Jean's shoes (PR-41, listed in LOC-05) lined up on the white mat, his duffel beside them. Background: sheer curtains hang still, and the city beyond shows no legible signage.

- **description**: SC17's opening insert, 4 silent seconds: the building's enclosed rear service bay at ground level, beside the service lift (the script's term; a freight elevator), about 07:00 ASSUMED. Used by building staff for deliveries ASSUMED; Venus parks the two-seater here, which the script logs as a convenience of the world. The car waits top down, clean, passenger seat empty. ## Looks

### VENUS_L01 (VENUS)

- **garment silhouette**: Closet: mid-to-high tier, bought plain and kept for years. She spends on fit, deep pockets and boots, never on a name. A tailor recut her jeans' back pockets to men's size. She dresses in the whites of her apartment plus pale denim. Story: Thursday 22 October 2026 (from PR-36's date, assumed), SC04-SC10. The city is at about 19 °C at night, so the jacket is on and open and her ankles are covered. She changes off screen in SC11 and hangs the jacket on PR-62, and she never wears it again in the pilot. Head to toe: a bone moleskin jacket, single-breasted, 4 buttons worn open, small point collar. It is waist length, with the hem level all round 1 cm above the top of the jeans waistband. It has two pockets: a right chest welt for the phone (PR-01) and an inside left pocket for the tin (PR-03). The sleeves are cut to mid-forearm with a 4 cm turned-back cuff. Under it is a chalk jersey shell with a high round neck, sleeveless with 7 cm shoulders, tucked in. Pale-blue high-rise bootcut jeans, full length, with a matched pair of oversized back pockets. A brown-black belt shows below the jacket hem. Brown-black pull-on ankle boots: 4 cm stacked heel, almond toe, shaft 3 cm above the ankle bone, worn under the jeans. Ecru ankle socks and an unlined bra, both hidden. Older than the story: the jeans.
- **materials colors**: Jacket: cotton moleskin 330 g/m², brushed face, matte, holds its shape; bone #D6CCBA (approx L*82, C*9). The fronts are half-lined in cotton drill 200 g/m², natural #D8CFBE, and the facings are moleskin. Buttons: bone corozo #D2C7B2, matte. Right chest welt: a 2 cm welt in the moleskin over a drill bag, with both lower corners bar-tacked. Behind it, the right chest is interfaced with 110 g/m² cotton fusible from the shoulder seam to 2 cm below the bag. Two 1 cm cotton twill stay tapes, natural #D8CFBE, run from the bag's top corners to the shoulder seam. Inside left pocket: a welt set across the facing and lining, over a drill bag bar-tacked at both bottom corners. Hanging loop: 5 cm of drill tape, #D8CFBE, at the inside back neck, for PR-62. A flat 80 g chain weight sits in each front's hem facing. Shell: cotton-silk single jersey 160 g/m², solid, chalk #EEE9DF (approx L*93). Plain jersey, because a fine rib would moiré and fight the 35mm grain. Jeans: rigid 100% cotton denim 12.5 oz/yd², light stonewash, pale blue #8FA3B8 (approx L*66, C*13, h 260°), as LOC-03 asks, sewn in tonal ecru thread. Back pockets: a matched pair, each with a single row of ecru topstitching around the edge and bar-tacked top corners. No decorative stitching, patch or tab. Hidden inside the right one is a sling in cotton drill 200 g/m², natural #D8CFBE (see fit). Front scoop pockets and bags as in VENUS_L03. Rivets and shank button are unstamped, gunmetal #5B5D60 approx, satin. Older than the story: the right back pocket is faded to #A9B9C9 in an L, the outline of the part of PR-02 that rides inside it. The L is a 17 x 3.5 cm band (the slide) whose lower edge runs 6 cm below the mouth, joined at the side-seam end by a 7 x 2.5 cm block rising to the mouth (the lower grip). The mouth is frayed to white threads for 8 cm at that end, where the grip rubs. To rebuild it, mask the L, sand and bleach-rub it, and sand the edge. It is a detail, not an identity mark, because models mirror. Belt: 3 cm bridle leather, brown-black #2E2724 (approx L*16, C*4), 5 holes, with a roller buckle in blackened steel #2F3032 approx, matte. Boots: calf leather, brown-black #2E2724, hidden elastic gore, no hardware; toes scuffed and re-polished, dark sole edge, no coloured sole. Socks: cotton, ecru #E4DED2. Bra: unlined, tan-brown #9C6A4C approx, matched to her skin. No logo, lettering or trade dress on any piece.
- **fit notes**: 177 cm, US women's 4/S, tall sleeve. Jacket: shoulder seam at the shoulder point, 10 cm ease at the chest. The fronts hang straight and open, 2 cm off the shell. The hem sits 1 cm above the waistband top and 7 cm above the back-pocket mouths. Right chest welt: a 9 cm horizontal opening, its top edge level with the armhole base (22 cm above the hem), centred 10 cm in from the front edge. The bag is 13.5 cm deep and 10 cm wide and ends 8.5 cm above the hem. From SC04 (as she leaves the car) to SC10, PR-01 (15 x 7.2 x 0.8 cm, about 200 g assumed) rides upright, screen-in, lens end down; its top 1.5 cm of bare black glass and frame shows above the welt (PR-01 S2). The stay tapes carry the weight to the shoulder, so the right front hangs plumb, its hem stays level with the left to within 1 cm, and the welt stays horizontal. Inside left pocket: the welt is 14 cm wide, 2 cm above the armhole base; the bag is 22 cm deep and ends 2 cm above the hem. PR-03 rides at the bottom; from SC08 the 20 cm PR-30 stands beside it, its top 2 cm under the welt. Phone and tin sit on opposite fronts, outside right and inside left, and never share a pocket. The jacket ends above her lap, so no sit fold crosses either pocket on the bar stool or in the rideshare, and the straw reaches SC12 uncreased. In SC09 his hands go in through the open fronts to the inside left pocket; the outside right welt stays shut on the phone. Cuff: the edge sits 12 cm above the wrist bone, so both wrists and hands read, and so do her three locked marks (right-hand scar, right-thumb nick, left-index burn): the SC05 forearm touch, the SC07 chimney and the S03 fingertip. In S03 no cuff comes within 30 cm of the straw house. Shell: 4 cm ease, neckline 2 cm below the collarbone notch. Jeans: 4 cm waistband with its top 2 cm below the navel, 2 cm ease at the hip, fitted through the thigh. Bootcut from the knee to a 23 cm opening; the hem sits 1.5 cm off the floor at the heel. Back pockets: each 19 cm wide x 15 cm deep, mouths 6 cm below the waistband top, inner edges 2 cm from the centre-back seam. Right pocket: a drill sling spans the full inner width with its bottom fold 6 cm below the mouth, its sides caught in the pocket's edge topstitching and its top under the pocket hem, so nothing new shows outside. PR-02 (17 x 12 x 3 cm, about 600 g, both assumed) rides slide down along the sling fold, grip up at the side-seam end, muzzle toward the centre back. 6 cm of grip stands above the mouth, its top level with the waistband top and 1 cm under the jacket hem, so the grip clears the jacket seated or reaching back (PR-02 S1). The bar tacks and belt carry the weight, so the waistband doesn't dip on the right. Belt on its middle hole in every look. The boots add 4 cm: she stands 181 cm, about 5 cm under JEAN in his shoes.
- **accessories**: Nothing worn. Ears, neck, wrists and both hands bare: no ring, watch or bracelet. Her hands touch the phone, his forearm, the straws and the tin (taste note: hands before faces).; Nails bare and short: 1-2 mm free edge, filed square, buffed.; Hand double (casting joint lock): the same bare nails, the three marks transferred, the same cuff landmark.; Set-owned: PR-01 in the right chest welt from SC04 to SC10 (set on PR-53 off screen in SC11). PR-03 in the inside left pocket, joined by PR-30 from SC08. PR-02 on the right back pocket's sling until the SC04 swap into the glovebox.; Car keys (PR-61) in the left front jeans pocket from SC04. No bag, no sunglasses.
- **hair makeup**: Skin: medium-deep, warm undertone, #A36A48 approx at the cheekbone (casting hex). No base. Pores stay open across the nose and inner cheeks. The three freckles on the left cheekbone and the laugh lines stay uncovered. No contour, highlighter or powder. Brows: heavy and straight, brushed up with clear gel; the right one sits 3 mm higher and is left that way. Eyes: brown-black kohl #2A211C approx pressed into the upper lash line only, with no wing, lower liner, mascara or lashes. Lips: bare, clear balm. Hair: dark brown #3A2417 approx, dense 3B curls with honey highlights #B98A55 approx from mid-length. Worn loose past the shoulder blades with a centre part, set wash-and-go with curl cream and diffused. The curls stay defined, never stretched or straightened. Never lightened, de-aged or smoothed by makeup, the grade or the 35mm look.
- **continuity locks**: [wide] Bone jacket open, all 4 buttons undone, hem 1 cm above the waistband, brown-black belt showing below it: bone over pale blue.; [medium, SC05-SC10] Right chest welt with 1.5 cm of black phone edge above it, on HER right; welt level, right front hanging plumb. A phone on her left means the frame is mirrored: reject it.; [medium and inserts] Cuff edge at mid-forearm, 12 cm above the wrist bone. Both wrists and hands bare; the right-hand scar, right-thumb nick and left-index burn uncovered.; [close-up] Centre part, curls loose. In every counter hand insert (SC05-SC08, S03) the hair sits behind both shoulders.; [close-up] Kohl on the upper lash line only, no wing. Lips bare.; [SC08-SC09 medium] Left front hangs straight, no drag at the inside pocket, no straw tip above its welt.; [SC04 insert, reaching back] Pistol grip up at the side-seam end of the right back pocket, 6 cm above the mouth, clear of the jacket hem. Both back pockets the same size, 19 cm wide.

### VENUS_L02 (VENUS)

- **garment silhouette**: Closet: the one thing at home she has worn for years. Story: SC12 into SC17, the apartment at about 21 °C, night into morning. She does all of SC16-SC17's labour in it: rolling JEAN over, pulling the fitted corner, dressing him, stripping and remaking the bed, kneeling to scrub PR-45. Change beat: after the flat straw lands on the pink cloth in PR-60 and before she closes the tin, she changes off screen into VENUS_L03. Head to toe: one oversized men's athletic T-shirt with raglan sleeves to the elbow and a crew collar rib with a V-notch at centre front, 81 cm from the high point of the shoulder to the front hem (80 cm at the back). Under it a skin-matched modesty short, never seen. Bare legs and bare feet. The raglan line and V-notch make it read as a gym or team shirt in a wide. Which team (Lucas's Raptors shirt) stays open until A10 clears it or a fictional team is designed and cleared (blocker 5). No team colour, logo, number or lettering. Older than the story: the T-shirt.
- **materials colors**: Combed cotton single jersey 180 g/m², solid, garment-washed. Body: ecru #E8E3D8 (approx L*90, C*4). Raglan sleeves, collar rib and V-notch: oatmeal #D2C9B7 (approx L*81, C*9, h 84°), so the diagonal from underarm to collar reads in a wide. Raglan seams twin-needle coverstitched in tonal ecru thread. Collar: 2 cm 1x1 rib with a 4 cm V-notch insert. No mesh, because it moirés. Older than the story: more than 100 washes. The collar rib ripples, the side seams twist 2 cm toward the front, and the front hem hangs 1 cm longer than the back. To rebuild it, enzyme-wash the tee and stretch the rib by hand. A second tee, aged the same way, stands by for SC17. Modesty short (production only): circular-knit nylon-elastane 190 g/m², no side seams, bonded edges, #9C6A4C approx to her skin. SC17 risk: PR-46 turns pink #E3A9A6, and cotton jersey takes the stain on contact and won't come clean between takes. Rule broken on purpose: the craft rule asks for a colour set apart from the set. Both tones sit 5-14 L* under the walls #F4F2EE and the sheets #F8F7F4, so she belongs to the white room, her skin and dark curls carry her read, and JEAN stays the only dark mass (verified: 'The colour theme is white'; tone: clean). Story-red check (no product): the drops #6E1C18 are approx L*24, C*43, h 34°. Ecru clears by chroma at C*4; oatmeal clears at C*9 and sits 50° away.
- **fit notes**: A men's XL tall on a US women's 4. Raglan sleeves, so no shoulder seam: the sleeve line runs from the underarm to the collar, and the cloth drops past her shoulder point. The oversized neck drops the garment's high point 2 cm below hers. 40 cm ease at the chest. The V-notch point sits 2 cm below the collarbone notch. The sleeve hem sits at the elbow, so both forearms and hands read: SC12 with the two glasses and her fingertip on the tin lid, SC15 folding his clothes and turning the speaker ring, SC16 her hand to her forehead and the roll, SC17 dressing him and scrubbing. Hem states, fit-checked on her (the hem is taken up or let down to hold the standing mark). Standing: front hem 10 cm above the top of the kneecap. Seated in PR-44 (SC12-SC13): about 20 cm above the kneecap across the lap. Carried (SC14): up to the hip crease on the outer thigh. Bent over the bed or kneeling forward to scrub (SC16-SC17): the back hem rises to the gluteal fold. The modesty short is cut for the worst of these, rise to the natural waist, legs ending 4 cm below the gluteal fold, so the garment, not the hem, keeps 'no nudity in any piece'; if its bonded edge enters frame it reads as skin. Lucas's 'no underwear' is implied, never shown. In bed (SC15-SC16) the tee stays on under the duvet.
- **accessories**: Nothing worn. Ears, neck, wrists, hands and feet bare.; Nails as VENUS_L01: bare and short.; Set-owned things she handles: the two PR-43 tumblers; PR-03, her fingertip on its lid; PR-30. SC15: his black clothes, folded square with nothing on top, and PR-54's volume ring, her hand passing PR-01 to reach it. SC16: the fitted sheet corner. SC17: his clothes as she dresses him, PR-04 out of PR-05's outside pocket (film director's ruling), the sheets, and PR-46, held in her right hand.
- **hair makeup**: Skin: medium-deep, warm undertone, #A36A48 approx, never lightened by makeup, the grade or the 35mm look. No base. Pores, freckles and laugh lines as VENUS_L01. The L01 kohl is still on, upper lash line only. Hair: dark brown #3A2417 approx, dense 3B curls with honey highlights #B98A55 approx, loose past the shoulder blades, centre part, never straightened. SC12-SC13: she has pushed the curls back behind both shoulders with her fingers; the hair tech resets them there between takes. SC15, from when she lies down: curls flattened at the back of the head. SC16, morning: crown frizzed into a loose halo, back flattened, kohl softened and smudged 2 mm below both lower lash lines, lips dry. No fresh makeup. SC17 up to the change beat: SC16's state holds unchanged. At the change she washes her face and puts her hair up, off screen (VENUS_L03).
- **continuity locks**: [wide] Ecru tee with oatmeal raglan sleeves, the underarm-to-collar diagonal reading. Bare legs and feet. JEAN is the only dark mass in the room.; [medium] V-notch at centre front of the oatmeal collar rib. No logo, number, lettering or team colour anywhere, front or back.; [insert] Sleeves end at the elbow. Both hands bare.; [SC12-SC13 tin and glass inserts] Curls behind both shoulders; no hair crosses PR-03, PR-30 or the PR-43 tumblers.; [SC16 close-up, and SC17 up to the change beat] Kohl smudged below both lower lash lines. Crown frizzed, back of the head flattened.; [SC17 scrub insert to the change beat] No pink anywhere on the ecru tee. PR-46 stays in her right hand, clear of hem and sleeves.; [SC17 wide] She is in the tee until the straw lands on the pink cloth in PR-60; the white shirt never appears before that beat.

### VENUS_L03 (VENUS)

- **garment silhouette**: Closet as VENUS_L01: the same jeans, belt and boots, with a fresh shirt. Story: Friday 23 October 2026 (assumed). Change beat: in SC17, after the flat straw goes into PR-60 and before she closes the tin, she changes off screen. The pressed shirt is the last thing in the flat she makes spotless. She leaves carrying the bone jacket, never wearing it, the tin in its inside left pocket (PR-03 S6). V01, V02 and S01: the open car, 27 °C in the late afternoon falling to 17 °C at dusk; no jacket, sleeves rolled because she is driving and filming. SC18: the motel at night, about 15 °C; only her hair changes. Head to toe: a white man-tailored button-front shirt, point collar, 7 buttons, the top two open and the third fastened at the sternum, tucked in all round, sleeves rolled in two 6 cm turns. With it the pale-blue bootcut jeans, brown-black belt, ankle boots and ecru socks from VENUS_L01. Pocket map from V01-B09: phone in the right front, keys in the left front, pistol in the right back. She wears no sunglasses of her own.
- **materials colors**: Shirt: cotton poplin 120 g/m², solid, matte, white #F2EEE6 (route palette; approx L*94, C*3). Buttons: shell-white corozo #EDE8DE. Poplin creases, so the creases are locked states. S1, from the SC17 change beat to the end of SC17: pressed, no creases. S2, from V01-B01: two horizontal creases across the front just above the belt (the seated fold) and one diagonal crease in each inner elbow above the roll. S2 holds unchanged through V01, V02, S01 and SC18 and is never pressed out between shots. The shirt stays clean: no pink, no dust. Jeans, belt, boots and socks as VENUS_L01: pale blue #8FA3B8 with unstamped gunmetal #5B5D60 approx satin rivets and the drill-slung, L-faded right back pocket; brown-black #2E2724 with the blackened-steel #2F3032 approx matte buckle; ecru #E4DED2. Front pockets: scoop mouths, bags in cotton twill 170 g/m², natural #D8CFBE, each faced with the denim 10 cm deep so no bag cloth shows at the mouth. Hair elastics: two, fabric-covered in ecru cotton #E2DBCB, no metal. Jacket (carried at the end of SC17 only): bone moleskin #D6CCBA. Story-red check (no product): the REC dot #D7261E is approx L*47, C*82, h 37°. The shirt (C*3) and the belt and boots (C*4, near its hue) clear by chroma; the denim is 223° away.
- **fit notes**: Shirt: shoulder seam at the shoulder point, 12 cm ease at the chest. Tucked 10 cm deep, so it stays in when she leans across JEAN's knees (V01-B09) and walks the frontage (B10). Unrolled, the sleeves end at the wrist bone. Two 6 cm turns put the roll's lower edge 12 cm above the wrist bone, the L01 cuff landmark, so the right-hand scar, right-thumb nick and left-index burn read the same at the bar and in the car. Hand double: the same roll and landmark, nails bare, marks transferred. Neckline open down to the third button at the sternum. From the first second of V01-B08, one open arm of PR-04 hooks over the placket edge above that button, lenses outside the shirt, until she unhooks it in SC18. Belt on its middle hole. Front pockets: scoop mouth 11 cm along the waistband and 8 cm down the side seam; bag 19 cm deep from the waistband top and 14 cm wide, its fly-side edge carried into a stay stitched into the fly, so a load hangs across the front instead of dragging the waistband. Right front, V01-B09 to SC18: PR-01 pushed fully in, its top 4 cm below the waistband top, nothing rising behind the 3 cm belt. Standing (from B10) the phone hangs upright and the denim shows only a flat 7 cm-wide outline (PR-01 S7); seated at the wheel it tips into the scoop's diagonal, below the door line. Left front: the PR-61 keys lie flat from B09. Right back: PR-02 on the drill sling as in VENUS_L01, 6 cm of grip showing, nothing covering it. Front bag and back pocket sit on opposite sides of the side seam and never overlap. SC18: wardrobe needs the elastics out and the hair down while she stands, and PR-01 out of the right front pocket before she sits; the order is the director's. Clear zone: no cuff, hair or strap enters the lower-right 15% of the phone frame; only her thumb does.
- **accessories**: Nothing worn. Ears, neck, wrists and both hands bare: no ring, watch or bracelet, and no elastic on either wrist. Her hands touch the phone, his collar, his chin and the glovebox (taste note: hands before faces).; Nails bare, short, filed square. The right-hand scar, right-thumb nick and left-index burn stay uncovered.; Set-owned: PR-01 in the right front jeans pocket from V01-B09 until she takes it out in SC18; PR-02 in the right back pocket from V01-B09 until it goes into PR-06 at the jamb in SC18 (PR-02 S4); PR-61 in the left front pocket from V01-B09, joined by the two elastics in SC18; PR-04 hooked in her neckline from V01-B08 until she unhooks it in SC18.; No sunglasses, hat, headscarf or bag worn. The bone jacket is carried, never worn, at the end of SC17.
- **hair makeup**: Skin: medium-deep, warm undertone, #A36A48 approx. Washed at the SC17 change: no base, kohl or powder. Pores, freckles and laugh lines show, and her own heat sheen on the forehead and nose is left alone. Brows brushed up with clear gel. Lips: clear balm. Hair: dark brown #3A2417 approx, dense 3B curls with honey highlights #B98A55 approx. From the SC17 change it is gathered into one low puff at the nape, about 18 cm across, held with the two ecru elastics and smoothed back from the hairline along a centre part with gel. Nothing hangs loose at the face or neck, so the wind can't blow hair across her hands or the phone. The puff holds unchanged through V01, V02 and S01 and into SC18. SC18, standing at her chair: the elastics come out, and the curls drop loose past the shoulder blades on the centre part, as at the bar, and lie on his black right shoulder where her head rests. Two marks of the day stay: the first 3 cm at the hairline flat with gel, and a soft kink across the lengths where the elastics sat. Curl texture never straightened; never lightened by makeup, the grade or the 35mm look.
- **continuity locks**: [car wide V01 34-42 s, and S01 from behind her right shoulder; everything above the 72 cm door line] White shirt, collar open to the third button, sleeves rolled with the lower edge 12 cm above the wrist bone, the dark low puff at the nape, no jacket.; [standing wide, V01-B10] White shirt tucked all round over pale denim, brown-black belt showing: white over pale blue.; [insert, phone-frame edges] Both wrists and hands bare, no elastic on either wrist.; [medium] Crease state: S1 pressed from the SC17 change beat to the end of SC17; S2 from V01-B01, two horizontal creases just above the belt and one at each inner elbow.; [close-up, from the SC17 change to SC18 until she stands at her chair] One low puff at the nape, centre part, no loose strands at the face or neck.; [SC18 front-camera frame] Curls loose over his black shoulder, centre part, hairline flat with gel.; [medium] Neckline bare in S01 and in V01 before B08. From B08's first second to SC18's unhooking: PR-04 hooked at the centre of the neckline, above the fastened third button.; [medium back and three-quarter, standing, V01-B10 to the SC18 jamb] Pistol grip 6 cm above the right back pocket at its side-seam end, shirt tucked clear of it. Right front: a flat phone outline, nothing above the pocket mouth or behind the belt. Left front: only a flat key outline.

### JEAN_L01 (JEAN)

- **garment silhouette**: Closet: mid-price and bought to be looked at. He spends on the shirt and the shoes and skimps on the trousers and belt, which he hasn't replaced since he got heavier. Everything he is ever photographed in is black. Story: Thursday 22 into Friday 23 October 2026 (assumed), SC04-SC15. The city is at about 19 °C at night and the bar and apartment are warm: one black outfit, no jacket. Head to toe: a black long-sleeve dress shirt, semi-spread collar on a 4 cm stand, no chest pocket, 7 buttons, top button open and the second fastened, cuffs buttoned, tucked in all round. Black flat-front trousers, mid rise, straight leg, half break. A brown-black belt on hole 1, nearest the buckle. Black mid-calf socks. Brown-black leather Derbies (PR-41). Plain black boxer briefs. SC15: clothes off (PR-56), bare back and arms, the white sheet at his waist. Older than the story: the belt. K08 wall-print rail (PR-08, PR-20: about 250 prints, June to October 2026, assumed), three variants that date the prints. K08-A, about 100 prints (40%), night bar-window and night street: this look (SH-1, TR-1, belt, PR-41) without the third-night creases; PR-08's top print, at the bar window, is K08-A. K08-B, about 100 prints (40%), June to August day prints at the pump and crossing streets: a black heavyweight crew tee, sleeves to mid-bicep, worn untucked over TR-1, black socks, PR-41. K08-C, about 50 prints (25 day, 25 dusk), October: a black hip-length overshirt worn open over the K08-B tee, with TR-1 and PR-41. That gives PR-20's half night, half day.
- **materials colors**: Shirt (SH-1): unmercerised cotton twill 150 g/m², lightly brushed, true matte, blue-black #24262B (approx L*15, C*4, h 276°). The nap holds PR-57's dust, so the grains, not the cloth, glint under the video light. Risk: the same nap grabs white bouclé fibre from the sofa, PR-44 and the rug, so shirt and trousers are lint-rolled between takes. Collar band 43 cm; the stays are gone. Buttons: matte black corozo #26272B.
