## Assumptions and blockers

### Development

- A01 | runtime | yes | The pilot runs about 30 minutes, as a half-hour drama | if_wrong: D01 → 3000 s (~50-min) hour-long outline with a B-story on Jean's ex and the dropped case
- A02 | story | yes | Jean is on Venus's list: his 'tough breakup' is with an ex whose domestic-violence report went nowhere | if_wrong: premise → Jean is innocent and her limerence alone chose him; the series turns on her misjudgment
- A03 | story | yes | Venus has watched Jean for months before the bar, so 'have we met before' is a test she already knows the answer to | if_wrong: premise → she picks him that night from a case file, and the motel wall is built after the kill
- A04 | story | yes | Decorum is her private verdict: she knows the true answers, and each lie costs him a straw | if_wrong: premise → the game is only flirtation, and the case file alone carries the verdict
- A05 | story | yes | Jean dies overnight in the white apartment from what is in the metal tin ('let's take a trip'), so the room stays spotless | if_wrong: premise → she uses the pistol off screen before dawn and the pilot never shows how
- A06 | story | yes | The pilot ends on the first photo of them together, pinned beside the first photos of the next man | if_wrong: premise ending → she takes the photo and burns Jean's wall; the next man arrives in episode 2
- A07 | route | yes | The route is commission: D01, V01 and V02 are pitch material for drama development execs | if_wrong: objective → festival selection; D01 → 12-min proof-of-concept short outline
- A08 | music | no | 'Trans/soul' means an original trance-soul track that Venus puts on herself, heard inside the scene | if_wrong: tone music line → licensed soul track, with the clearance cost flagged on the white-apartment beat only
- A09 | route | yes | V01, V02 and the stills are generated end to end | if_wrong: V01 → hybrid, with live-action performances on a parked-car rig composited over generated desert plates
- A10 | legal | no | Real brands in the idea (the Uber, the Raptors shirt) appear unbranded on screen. Source: Lucas's script. Scope: on-screen logos in the pilot and the pitch pieces. Owner: the producer. Status: pending, nothing cleared. | if_wrong: must_avoid → drop the brand line and add clearance to the budget
- A11 | visual | no | The car is the vintage open two-seater from refs 09 and 11, so her laughter and his stillness share one open frame | if_wrong: V01 → an older closed sedan, with the ride played through the windshield
- A12 | spec | no | Pitch video is delivered at 16:9, and V02 is a 9:16 rebuild from V01 footage | if_wrong: V01 → 2.39:1 letterbox, with V02 reframed from it
- A13 | story | yes | The dried blood behind the toilet and the red drops on the floor belong to the previous man, from a night the tin failed and she used the pistol | if_wrong: premise → the blood is hers, and the untaken meds and the blood are one secret
- A14 | story | yes | The white apartment is where she lures each man; the motel is where she builds his wall and brings him afterwards | if_wrong: premise → the white apartment is staged for the date and the motel is her only home
- A15 | production | no | V01 needs 3 locations (desert road and town, motel exterior, motel room), 2 on-camera roles and 2 consistent characters, 1 voice (her laughter, no words), 0 spoken or lip-synced lines, an estimated 15–20 generated shots, 3 hand shots (phone, pistol, groceries) and no product reference | if_wrong: V01 → if Venus must speak, she speaks from behind the phone with her face unseen, so no lip-sync is needed
- A16 | production | yes | The make-or-break shot is Jean's pale open eyes revealed while Venus keeps laughing. The first test is a 5 s shot in a moving convertible in which a still, open-eyed passenger holds consistency. | if_wrong: V01 → the reveal moves to the parked car at the motel, where a static frame holds him still
- A17 | story | yes | The season question is the one man who passes decorum | if_wrong: format_rationale → closed procedural, one man per episode
- A18 | story | yes | The tin sits closed on the table until Jean gives his line about love; opening it is her sentence, so she was hoping he would pass | if_wrong: premise → Jean opens the tin as soon as she slides it over, and his line only confirms what she had already decided

### Strategy

- Market and language: English-language drama for North American streamers and networks. This is inferred from the North American brands in Lucas's idea and is not confirmed.
- Client and money: there is no brand. The client is Lucas as creator, and LIMERENCE is the working title (development's working brand). The revenue path is a paid script commission or development deal, then a series order. Subscriber value downstream belongs to the buyer.
- Release cadence (weekly, or all episodes at once) is the buyer's call. H9 tests return from episode to episode, not a weekly schedule.
- Reach: V01 and D01 go privately from the producer or agent to a named list of execs, with no media buy. V02 and S03 go public only if the producer decides a public teaser helps the pitch.
- Viewing conditions: V01 is watched on a laptop with sound on. V02 must read muted. S03 is muted in-feed.
- Evidence: Lucas's raw idea text, including 'whose case get lost', is not used as evidence. F13 (the logline) carries 'the man the system lost' as content. Because the old F15 was dropped, the former F16-F19 are now F15-F18, and the ending proof point is now F17. F19 and F20 quote development's format_rationale and the D01 label as content only, as the reviewer directed.
- Proposition contingent on: A02 (Jean's ex's domestic-violence report went nowhere) for 'the man the system lost' in the pilot. F13 supports it for the series as a whole.
- Proposition contingent on: A18 (the tin stays closed until his line about love). It shows she hoped he would pass, which keeps her love real rather than a trap.
- Proof points are held to four. F2 is not a standalone proof point: F17's ending pays F2 off and carries 'can't make him', while F2 still does work in product_relevance, S02 and the competitor fallback. F18 restates F1's support in story terms. F3, F5, F14-F16, F19 and F20 are story mechanics or season content rather than proof of the proposition.
- Proof points leave out F4 and F7-F12 (gun, colour, meds, blood, music). They are execution, or they argue a second idea (her instability), and under must_avoid the meds must never read as the reason.
- Sample sizes are consistent: 5 development readers for H2, H5 and success criterion 4, 6 viewer calls for H1, and 5 cold viewers for H4 and H9.
- Comparable titles in H3 are common knowledge, unverified and internal only. The phrase 'female Dexter' stays out of all public-facing material.
- Success thresholds are studio go/no-go lines, not market benchmarks. No statistic is claimed anywhere.
- Taste notes, which the file flags as example notes and not Lucas's own: the proposition follows 'Restraint over explanation' (it names her want, never her code) and 'Dry over sentimental', and breaks none. 'The product is a tool, then a hero' does not apply because there is no product.
- Blocker (Lucas): Alternative: key_message ('Venus can punish any man the system lost, but her strong desire for reciprocation is the one thing she can't satisfy: no man she hunts ever loves her back.') is not carried as the proposition. The main reason: 'no man she hunts ever loves her back' contradicts A17, the season question D01 must carry (the one man who tells the truth at decorum and seems to love her back). It is also a 29-word line with two beats, and 'any man' and 'the one thing' are not supported by any fact. Proposed instead: 'Punishing the man the system lost can't make him love her back.' This leaves the season question open. → Approve the 12-word proposition, or keep key_message as pitch copy with 'any', 'the one thing' and 'ever' removed so it no longer rules out A17.
- Blocker (Producer): The competitor check (H3) has not been run. No one has checked the clause only LIMERENCE owns (she falls for every man she sentences, and the sentence is how she keeps him) against the nearest threats, You, Killing Eve and Promising Young Woman, or against current and announced slates. The comps stay internal. → Before the first send, check those three titles first, then scan current and announced slates and loglines. If one matches, sharpen the proposition to the wall with no photo of them together (F2).
- Blocker (Lucas / Producer): Permission is not established: there are no credits, attached producer or prior work that an exec could check. → Supply one checkable credential for the cover note (a produced short, an attached producer or showrunner, a festival selection), or confirm there is none.
- Blocker (Lucas): A02 is unconfirmed. Whether Jean is guilty decides whether 'the man the system lost' holds for the pilot. → Confirm A02. If Jean is innocent, the proposition becomes 'Punishing the man she chose can't make him love her back.' and H1 is retested.
- Blocker (Producer): There are no flight dates, no exec send list and no decision on releasing V02 and S03 publicly. Without them, the success windows, the rate denominator and whether social metrics apply cannot be set. → Supply send dates, the list size and a decision on public release. The client sets targets or accepts the labeled studio thresholds.

### Routes

- The three most familiar openings in this genre (internal, no titles named): (1) a vigilante killer's cold open with a voiceover laying out the code; (2) the femme-fatale seduction with slow-motion glamour and a last-scene reveal that she is the killer; (3) a true-crime pastiche with case-file cards, news audio and 'unsolved' titles. All three routes avoid 1 and 3. R1 subverts 2: her touch asks rather than controls. R2 subverts the killer's trophy photo. R3 subverts 2 by making the seduction game a chance rather than a trap.
- Ten one-liners were drafted; the three kept differ in reframe, device and structure. R1: linear, objective camera, the hand as device; its idea costs 'a killer's touch is control'. R2: told through her phone's frame, widening at the reveal, the front camera as device; it costs 'the photos are trophies'. R3: two braided timelines, omniscient POV, the straws as device; it costs 'the seduction game is a trap'. [changed] R1's previous idea ('The kill is already over; the only suspense left is whether he'll love her back') restated the development premise, so every route's example served it. It failed the pairing test and was replaced. Other cut drafts: 'the sentence is the first day he can't walk out' (reads as control, against F1) and 'a dead man can't lie at decorum'.
- There is no product. product_role describes the job of the device that carries the proposition and Venus's role in frame. The taste note 'the product is a tool, then a hero' does not apply.
- All beats are tagged G or C: V01, V02 and the stills are generated end to end (A09), no shoot budget is authorised and no renders were supplied, so no beat is tagged P or R. Every reveal beat depends on the A16 test shot.
- Creative additions not found in Lucas's idea or the facts, for Lucas to approve: the dead traffic light, the dust devil with her arm catching his weight, and the unreturned squeeze (R1); the stop sign nobody else will reach, the REC timer, the flip-camera icon, the under-a-second front-camera flip, the phone's video light, turning his chin with two fingers, and the battery lantern (R2); the bent straw in his pocket and the wind lifting it (R3); the oranges (all routes).
- [changed] R2, question for Lucas: it changes his cold open's camera. 40 of 90 s are seen inside her phone's upright frame (0-34 s and 50-56 s); her face is withheld until 34 s and Jean's until 42 s; the pilot's ending (A06) becomes her first use of the front camera for a photograph. The fallback's under-a-second flip is a live preview, not a photograph, so A06's 'first photograph of them together' still stands. No beat is dropped.
- [changed] R2's time and light hold together: the recording runs from REC 00:00:04 in low gold sun to 00:58:40 at blue dusk and 01:04:55 on his eyes. The third hand is gone: she fixes his collar only when stopped at the stop sign, and she only films or touches him one hand at a time while driving.
- [changed] R3, question for Lucas: braiding a silent bar night into V01 spends the pilot's flashback. The braid now stops with one straw still standing. The last straw is his line about love in the white apartment (F16), and the tin opens only after it (A18); both stay in D01. Lucas's bar dialogue stays in D01 because V01 has no spoken lines (A15). Decorum as a verdict rests on A04.
- V01 in every route never shows or implies how Jean died: no wound, no tin, no method. The only blood rule applies to the white apartment, which is in D01 and not in V01.
- The car is the vintage open two-seater (A11). Silver is used in R1's palette to sit against gold and black. Red (ref 11) is production design's call and does not change the routes.
- [changed] Every beat runs at real speed, and no route uses an aerial or drone transition. R1's 24-32 s is now the dust devil and her arm catching his weight; R3's 44-50 s is the wind lifting the straw from his pocket. There is no nudity or sex in V01, V02 or the stills. Nothing on screen is branded; clearance is still pending (A10).
- [changed] Rights: R2's phone UI (REC dot, running timer, flip-camera icon) is an original design with no OS or maker's marks. Source: this route. Scope: V01, V02 and S01. Owner: production design. Status: clearance pending with the producer under A10, not claimed as cleared. If it reads as a real operating system, simplify to the REC dot and timer and play the hesitation as the front-camera flip.
- [changed] R2's season extension, a proposal for Lucas that is not yet in D01: the front camera turns on only once per man, at the table, afterwards, so the season question (A17) can become the man she turns it on while he is still alive.
- Venus and Jean are fictional, and neither is modelled on a face in Lucas's references. The references are cited only as the brief describes them; no reference was inspected in this run.
- Taste notes followed in all routes: hands before faces (Venus's first face at 32 s in R1, 34 s in R2 and 58 s in R3; Jean's head stays turned to the door, showing only collar, jaw and sunglasses, until his eyes, the one face shot); sound design before music (no score in V01; the trance-soul track belongs to the white apartment in D01); restraint over explanation (no voiceover and no text beyond the title); one real detail per scene (R1 the dead traffic light, R2 the stop sign, R3 the bent straw). [changed] R2's camera-roll scroll is cut because the wall already says none of them are together (F2); 82-88 s is now a held frame. Bent on purpose: R2's 18-26 s fallback shows her lower face for under a second before 34 s. R3's braid still asks the viewer to link the straws to the drive, which leans toward explanation, but it no longer shows the last straw fall, so the bend is smaller and is accepted as the brave route's cost.
- 'Female Dexter' and the comparable titles stay internal and appear in no route or deliverable.
- [changed] Revision log for this round: R2 repeats the flip hesitation after the eyes (50-56 s) and the rationale quotes it; the no-thumb fallback is replaced by the front-camera flip; the third hand is removed (stop sign); the timer matches the light; Jean's face is held back until 42 s; the camera-roll scroll is cut; the recurring device is restated; S01 is recomposed; the phone UI's rights are named. R1's idea is replaced and both squeeze beats are tagged with fallbacks. Aerials are cut in R1 and R3. R3's braid stops with one straw standing, and its V02 opens on a straw being drawn out.
- Blocker (Lucas): The recommendation quotes the strategy's proposition ('Punishing the man the system lost can't make him love her back.'), which Lucas has not approved yet (strategy blocker 1). For the pilot it also rests on A02 (Jean's guilt). → Approve the proposition or the trimmed key_message, and confirm A02. All three routes hold either way; only the rationale's quote changes.
- Blocker (Generation supervisor): Every route's reveal depends on the untested A16 shot: a still, open-eyed passenger in a moving open car who stays consistent across shots. → Run the 5 s A16 test before building V01. If it fails, every route moves the eyes reveal to the parked car at the motel, and V02 is recut from that shot.

### Script

- The craft brief asks for each want to be written in what_happens as a verb and an object, and earlier notes asked for the 'X wants' labels to come out of the exec text. Both are met: the D01 prose carries each want as action or a purpose clause, and the explicit wants, tactics and details sit in the crew annex in decisions.
- SC18 blocking: she sits at his right, the side his face is turned away from, and holds the phone in front of them and a little to her right. The front camera catches her face toward the lens and his face in three-quarter, his open eyes aimed off to his left, past the lens. Her two fingers lift toward his chin and stop, because turning him to the lens would break the performance rule's meaning (she sets him once and leaves him). The imperfect first photo is kept on purpose.
- SC16 shows Jean's dead face on the pillow at about 24 minutes into D01, after the cold open has already shown it. The V01 rule that his eyes are the one face shot is untouched.
- The fill slip is dated five weeks before the dash cam's date in SC09, which production design sets. A four-week organizer still full, plus a five-week-old fill, reads as 'untaken for weeks' with no drug name.
- If the V01-B04 or B08 fallbacks are used, part of her face appears in her own phone for under a second before SC18, which softens the claim that no in-world camera holds her face until SC18. A06, the first photograph of them together, still stands, because those fallbacks are live video and not a photograph.
- SC08 keeps the word 'trophy' as Jean's want, not hers: he collects women, and she keeps nothing of him except the SC18 photograph.
- The proposition line on the FRONT page ('punishing the man the system lost can't make him love her back') is marked pending until Lucas approves it.
- 'Camera department needs to be full 10s' is read as a quality bar. It might instead mean every generated shot is rendered as a full 10 s clip; the script allows that, because no V01 or V02 beat runs longer than 10 s.
- The 35mm film look covers the full-width picture in V01 and V02 and all three stills. The phone's upright frame stays phone video as a deliberate contrast.
- The scene timings in the D01 headings are estimates for a roughly 30-minute half hour (A01).
- Off-screen logistics: she fetches the two-seater from outside the bar between SC16 and SC17, and it waits in the building's enclosed rear service bay beside the service lift. Jean goes down in the service lift into the passenger seat between SC17 and SC01. The move itself is never shown.
- Creative additions in D01 for Lucas to approve or strike: the FRONT page; the pistol traded for the tin, the incident report and the prints swept into the glovebox (SC04); Jean's black duffel (SC04-SC10, SC17); the flat straw house and the tab slip (SC05); Jean's lie and 'That's a drink.' (SC06); the chimney straw and her fingertip on it (SC07); the coupe on the base straw and her photo of him alone (SC08); the ride booked on his phone, the corner she types, the seat behind the driver and the dash cam that never gets her face (SC09); 'Mind the step' and the shoes on the white mat (SC10); the wet tissue (SC11); the speaker's dial and her hands going still on the glasses, and the straw in her ice (SC12); the wet mark (SC13); the hand-washing that never meets the mirror and the dial turned up (SC15); 'J. Stay for breakfast.', the roll onto his back and the fitted corner (SC16); the service bay, the sunglasses, the organizer with its fill slip and the vial, the scrubbed drops and the flat straw in the trash (SC17); the printer, the second chair, the courthouse-steps photos and her fingers stopping short of his chin (SC03, SC18); and the season engine's escalation.
- Additions in V01 beyond the route's execution example: the pistol taken from the glovebox (56-60 s), the sunglasses hooked into her neckline (50-51 s), and the printer, second chair, bedroll and empty jugs along the far wall (72-90 s). She shoulders open a swollen door in place of Lucas's 'breaks into the motel'.
- Lucas's dialogue is kept, with light spelling and grammar cleanup. The trust/lust exchange is trimmed, not rewritten.
- 'Naked and Famous' is treated as a cocktail name, not a brand. The producer confirms this under A10.
- The phone UI (REC dot, timer, flip-camera icon) is an original design added in finishing. Its clearance is pending with the producer (A10) and is not claimed as cleared.
- The FRONT and ENGINE pages are rows in the beat sheet because the output carries one beat sheet; the 18 scenes SC01-SC18 are the episode. The crew annex sits in decisions for the same reason.
- Jean, Venus, Jean's ex, the next man and every case are fictional. No character is modelled on a face in Lucas's references. No reference image was inspected in this run; they are cited only as the brief describes them.
- The phrase Lucas used as an internal comparison does not appear in any deliverable.
- Blocker (Director / generation lead): A16: Jean's open, unblinking eyes in a moving convertible (V01 42-56 s; V02 4-13 s) have not been tested for consistency. The glovebox reach across his knees (V01-B09) is a second shot that depends on a hand holding up. → Run the 5 s test shot. If it fails, use the written fallback and play V01-B07 with the car parked at the motel. If the glovebox reach fails, use the B09 fallback, with the pistol already in her pocket. No story beat changes.
- Blocker (Camera department / Lucas): Two things need confirming against Lucas's intent: what 'full 10s' means, and how far the 35mm film look extends (full-width picture and stills, but not the phone frame). → Confirm the reading. If 'full 10s' means 10 s renders, cut each beat from inside one 10 s clip. If the phone frame should also be on film, grade it that way. The script needs no change either way.
- Blocker (Production design): Production design has updates that follow from this revision. LOC-07 gains the enclosed rear service bay (roller door, high window, service lift). PR-09's stamp reads NO CHARGES FILED. PR-23's empty jugs stand along the far wall. PR-50/PR-51 become a full four-week organizer with a pharmacy fill slip taped inside the lid (dated five weeks before the SC09 dash-cam date, no drug name) and an amber vial with its label to the wall. New props: a small white speaker with a physical dial on the bedroom nightstand (SC12, SC15, SC16), and the SC09 dash-cam frame with a running date and timestamp. → Update the world bible to match the script word for word. No beat depends on anything beyond these changes.
- Blocker (Producer): The meds beat in SC17's clean-up, now with the dated fill slip, still needs the mental-health human review the casting bible flagged. → Review SC15-SC17 as revised: the meds are seen, never named, never shown before her choice in SC15, and dated only by the slip. Strike or adjust the SC17 shelf-wipe if the review asks.
- Blocker (Producer): Clearance for the phone UI and the brands is pending (A10). → If the UI reads as a real operating system, keep only the REC dot and timer, and play the hesitation as the under-a-second front-camera flip (the fallbacks in V01-B04, V01-B08 and V02-B06). Confirm that 'Naked and Famous' needs no clearance.
- Blocker (Lucas): The creative additions, the FRONT page's proposition line, the season engine's escalation and the stylization log for the dusk chin turn need Lucas's approval. → Approve or strike each item. Any of them can go without losing a turn except five: the last-straw chain (SC07-SC08, SC12-SC13, SC17), which carries A18 and the season question; the speaker planted in SC12 and turned up in SC15, which carries Venus's climactic choice; the roll onto his back in SC16, which is that scene's turn; the duffel, the only source for V01-B11's bag; and the dash-cam seat in SC09, which keeps her out of every frame until SC18.

### Cast

- JEAN's body rule (changed this round, per all three review notes). The rigor one-block rule is struck ('neck fixed', 'head and shoulders move only together, as one block', 'her two fingers turn the block'), and rigor no longer appears anywhere in the bible as a cause. The bible now carries the treatment's rule: his head moves only when her hand moves it, stays where she leaves it, and never lolls or bobs. In V01-B07 the head turns alone on the neck, about 60°, with the torso square and the collar moving only where the jaw drags it. The script logs the dusk turn as deliberate stylization, not physiology, so casting plays no medical detail. The loose arm in SC16 and the arms that still bend in SC17 are kept as performance notes. The exact angles (about compass 30° after the B07 turn, and the SC18 gaze set of about 210°, both cited in the review notes) belong to the camera plan. Casting quotes them and doesn't re-derive them.
- SC18 staging. Two review notes ask for 'in SC18 her push does not move it', but the current script has no push: her two fingers lift toward his chin, then stop, and she takes the photo anyway. The bible follows the script. The rule holds either way: if the director restores a push, it does not move the head.
- VENUS's hand set is locked by the film director's ruling: the 2 cm scar and the 6 mm thumb nick on the right hand, and the 1 cm burn on the back of the left index finger, all pale #C99D80. The burn carries her identity in V01-SH02 and V02-SH01, the first time any part of her is on screen (taste note: hands before faces). The 'proposal' wording is removed.
- Hand-shot mapping, taken from the camera-plan citations in the review notes (the camera plan itself is not in this prompt). Right-thumb nick, on the flip icon: V01-B04 (SH07), V01-B08 (SH17), V02-B06 and SC18. Right-hand scar: V01-B07, the glasses lift and two-finger head turn (SH14-SH15); V02-B03; V01-B08, the sunglasses hook (SH16); SC18, on the phone. Left-index burn: V01-B02, the collar brush (SH02), and V02-B01 (SH01). The S03 fingertip and SC04's glovebox hand are the director's pick, and the locked marks show on whichever hand appears.
- Car side. The script now writes left-hand-drive geometry: the driver's seat sits on JEAN's left, and in SC18 'at the table, that is his left'. JEAN's mole and his further-receding temple are therefore locked on his left, the side facing the driver, and VENUS's one-frame read holds: left hand on the wheel, right hand holding the phone toward him. What remains is render drift (see blockers).
- The meds moved upstream. They now appear only in SC17's clean-up (the organizer full, the fill slip dated, the vial's label to the wall). In SC15 she washes her hands without meeting the mirror and the cabinet stays shut. The flat, padlock-check register is carried from the old SC15 note to SC17.
- SC16 and SC17 changed upstream, and the performance notes follow them. In SC16 she waits, then rolls him and turns his face on the pillow; this is where the head rule starts in story order. SC17 adds the drops she missed: one beat of stillness, then she scrubs. In SC15 her hand passes the phone without resting on it. That is the one time her rest-and-slide gesture is skipped, and it marks the choice with no face played.
- Setting. The script's decisions place the story in the US Southwest ('NO CHARGES FILED' wording) and set US vocabulary and spelling for every department, so the bible now uses both (windshield, organizer, color). The '-American' heritages, the US sizes and the assumed alcohol minimum of 25 rest on that setting. If Lucas relocates the story, the heritages are restated for the new country with no change to any anchor.
- JEAN's alive register is split at the lobby. In SC05-SC09 he is really lifted, so the flirt plays for real and the season question keeps its doubt. The habit tells start in SC10 and peak in the SC13 speech, which Lucas marks as a BS answer. The SC06 lie stays tell-free.
- JEAN's hair is settled (joint lock, stylist): tight 4A texture, dense, with the top at 12-15 cm stretched length, picked out at the crown so it moves as one mass. The A16 test confirms it, and the texture never changes after Lucas approves heritage.
- 35mm look (Lucas's relayed request, applied by the script). The casting consequences are three. Skin hex values are set under neutral light before the grade. The grade and grain never lift JEAN's skin to signal death. The micro anchors are sized to read in close-ups through grain and phone compression, and wide shots rely on the macro anchors.
- The cast is exactly the script's four IDs: VENUS, JEAN, BARTENDER and DRIVER. The next man in the courthouse-steps prints (SC18, ENGINE) appears in photos only and is not cast. Production design supplies a fictional man who shares neither JEAN's build nor his hair and resembles no real person. JEAN's ex (name blacked out) is never on screen. There is no voiceover character.
- Alcohol rule. No market code was given, so the minimum is assumed to be 25 and every visible face reads 30+. Targets are 34, 38, 50 and 61, and the bar background patrons in SC04-SC08 also read 30+.
- Heritage. All four are casting choices, not script requirements, and none goes into an anchor or a prompt. JEAN's reason is his name. VENUS is matched to him so the killing doesn't read as a cross-racial story. BARTENDER and DRIVER are cast apart from each other and from the leads. A proposal for D01: the men she sentences vary in heritage across the season, so 'the man the system lost' is never tied to one heritage.
- Lucas's 'tall slim model straight off your latest Vogue cover' is kept as height, slimness and bearing, not a glossy finish, because the tone rejects the glossy femme fatale. His optional blonde highlights are kept as a joint lock with the stylist.
- 'Pale' on JEAN comes only from desaturation, grey-ash at the lips and lids, dust, a dry grey-filmed cornea and eyes that never blink. It never comes from a lighter skin or iris hex.
- Squaring objects belongs to VENUS alone, as the clean-horror tell. BARTENDER puts things down off-angle, and JEAN turns his stool to her rather than squaring up.
- No reference was inspected; I worked only from the brief's descriptions. To stay clear of them, JEAN has no locs and no arm tattoo (ref 06), VENUS is not pale or auburn (ref 04), and no one is modeled on any face in refs 01-11.
- The script has no child. No one is flagged for visible difference, religious dress, pregnancy or age 70+ (DRIVER's target is 61). Mental health is flagged for human review in the blockers.
- Taste notes. 'Hands before faces': VENUS's locked hand marks carry her before her face appears at 34 s, and JEAN's neck mole holds him before his face at 42 s. 'Restraint over explanation': no knowing look to the lens, no menace register, the organizer played flat, and no physiology played on the dead body. 'Dry over sentimental': her verdicts and 'J. Stay for breakfast.' land at one dry pitch. Casting breaks no note on purpose; S01's face was already accepted upstream.
- Blocker (Generation lead, with the producer): VENUS's hand set is ruled (right-hand 2 cm scar and 6 mm thumb nick, left-index 1 cm burn), but K03-HND-VENUS and the hand-double brief still carry the old set: a left-thumb nick and no burn. The shot mapping comes from the review notes' citations, not from the camera plan itself. → The generation lead re-locks K03-HND-VENUS on the ruled set, all pale #C99D80, before the stage day and before any hand shot renders (V01-B02, B04, B07, B08; V02-B01, B03, B06; SC18). The producer briefs the hand double on the same set. The generation lead checks the mapping against the camera plan. Any render showing a left-thumb nick or a missing left-index burn is discarded.
- Blocker (Cinematographer, with the generation lead and the film director): Documents written to the old one-block rule may still carry it: the camera plan's V01-SH15 action and assumption (b), and any K03 or A16 test prompt that says 'fixed neck' or has head and shoulders turning together. → Restate each one to the bible's rule: the head turns alone on the neck, about 60°, with the torso and shoulders square and the collar moving only where the jaw drags it. The head stays where her hand leaves it and never lolls or bobs. The film director confirms the SC18 staging (the script's fingers stopping short, or a push that doesn't move the head).
- Blocker (Production design, with the generation lead): The script's geometry is left-hand drive, but generators often mirror a vintage two-seater to right-hand drive. That would put JEAN on her left and his left-side mole out of her phone's view from 0 to 42 s. → Lock the car reference in K03 as left-hand drive and discard any mirrored render. If right-hand drive is ever chosen on purpose, apply the mirror rule before locking. JEAN's mole, his further-receding temple and the visible sunglasses arm move to his right, VENUS's hand marks stay, and the camera department re-checks S01's over-the-right-shoulder composition.
- Blocker (Producer): Rights and consents have no owner yet. Source: this casting bible. Scope: (a) VENUS's laugh, the only voice in V01, V02 and D01; (b) the hand double for the hand shots in blocker 1; (c) if D01 is shot live action (A09 if_wrong), an intimacy coordinator and every performer's written consent for the SC07 insert, SC09 and SC14-SC16. Status: pending. Nothing is cleared, no performer is attached, and no one's availability, likeness or voice is claimed. → Generate the laugh, or record it from a consenting fictional-cast performer; no voice is cloned from a real person. Engage the hand double on a written release covering V01, V02 and SC18. Book the intimacy coordinator before any live-action D01 schedule. Record each clearance before the pitch pieces circulate.
- Blocker (Generation lead, with the producer): Generated faces can land on a recognisable real person, and S01 and V02 travel without D01's context. → Before S01 or V02 leaves the team, run a real-person resemblance check on the locked VENUS and JEAN faces, and on BARTENDER and DRIVER before any D01 material circulates. If a face reads as a real person, regenerate it within the same anchors and check again. Record the result with the producer. Status: pending.
- Blocker (Producer): No market alcohol code was given, and the bar, the drinks and the tin put every visible adult under the alcohol rule. → Confirm the target markets. Until then the cast assumes a minimum of 25, and every visible face, bar background included, reads 30+. If any market sets a higher minimum or bans showing people drinking, re-age the cast or recut the bar scenes and S03.
- Blocker (Producer, with a sensitivity reader): The script pairs a killer with medication she hasn't taken in weeks, and Lucas framed it as mental health. That is the representation risk the must-avoid list names (meds treated as the explanation for the killings), and the craft brief flags disability, which can include mental illness, for human review. The meds now appear in SC17 only. → Get a human or sensitivity review of SC17's organizer beat and of how D01 frames her meds before the pitch pieces circulate. Casting holds the beat to the padlock-check register (no wince, no pause on the compartments or the slip) and never ties her performance to a diagnosis. The reviewer confirms this or asks for changes.
- Blocker (Lucas (creative approval)): Heritage for all four characters is a casting choice, not a script requirement. The pieces that circulate raise the stakes: S01 and V02 make a dead Black man the key image beside a laughing woman, and they travel without D01's context. → Lucas approves or reassigns the heritages with S01 and V02 roughs in front of him, not from this text alone. If anything changes, redo skin hex, hair texture and JEAN's dead-state notes together, keep VENUS and JEAN matched or say why not, and restate the heritages if the setting moves from the US Southwest.
- Blocker (Director / generation lead, with the stylist): JEAN's dead anchors depend on the A16 test. In a moving convertible, under the 35mm look, a still, open-eyed passenger must hold his identity, his dead eyes (upper lid at the top edge of the iris, a dry grey-filmed cornea, dusted lashes), a head that holds where she set it with no loll or bob, and hair that moves, because V01-B07 and V02-B04 prove death with 'the wind moves his hair'. → Run the 5 s A16 test on the locked anchors with the 35mm look applied. It passes if the top of his hair shifts as one mass and the crown ends flutter within the 5 s, while his head holds its set angle, his torso stays square and his eyes don't blink. If the top moves too little, the stylist picks it out further within 12-15 cm and never changes the 4A texture. If the pallor or the dry cornea doesn't read through the grain, carry his death through dust, unblinking eyes and a desaturated grade, never a lighter hex, and use the parked-car fallback.

### World

- Every hex is a pre-grade value under an even key. The 35mm look, lenses and lighting belong to the camera department. The smallest marks (3-5 mm drops, 1 mm pin holes, the 6 mm lens smear) are sized for close views. Every prop state is keyed to a script beat, not to a shot.
- Place and year: an unnamed US Southwest city and desert, 2026 (ASSUMED), on the script's one continuous timeline. Locks: right-hand traffic, left-hand-drive cars, rear license plates only with every plate blank, English signage, a red octagon 'STOP', NEMA 5-15 outlets in the 2026 building, and two-slot NEMA 1-15 outlets in the 1958 motel.
- US vocabulary and spelling throughout, per the script's decision, because these words go straight into generation prompts: hood, fenders, windshield, trunk, curb, tire, license plate, organizer, color, center, gray, galvanized, two-story, outlets, medicine cabinet, roller shade, step trash can, weekender, pants. A few of the script's own words are kept verbatim: 'service lift' and 'roller door' (SC17), each glossed once in LOC-11 as a freight elevator and a roll-up door, plus 'tap' and 'basin' (SC11, SC15).
- Every dimension, weight, age and tenure is ASSUMED from its category, since none is verified. That covers the car section, the rooms, the tin, the printer, the prints, the bag weights, the 1958 motel, about five weeks at the motel, about two years in the apartment, and the desert loop.
- The car section is locked for its reference model (A09): cushion tops 35 cm, dash top 88 cm, windshield header 118 cm with no rearview mirror, pad tops 108 cm, eye lines Venus 110 cm and Jean 103 cm. These are design values, not measurements, and any change comes back to production design.
- P2 is built to option (b), and the SC18 left/right blocker is closed. P2 is car-frame 240° (compass about 330° heading north), a head-only turn with the torso square in P1 and P2. At the SC18 table, facing south, it becomes about 150°, 30° to his own left. The check for the reviewers: from Jean's head (x 108, y 100) to the phone over the PR-57 wrist arc (x 20-30, y 45-65), the offsets run dx -78 to -88 and dy -35 to -55, which is 22-35° left of straight ahead. The line runs 28.5° left (car-frame 241.5°) to the arc's center and 32° left (238°) to its near corner, with the lens a few cm above the wrist. The reviewers' 33° / 237° sits inside that spread, and 240° splits it. This replaces the package review's 300° / 210°, which turned his face 30° away from her and broke V01-B07 ('toward her'), B08's frontal eyes (A16) and SC18 ('his left').
- LOC-03 is re-keyed to P2. Jean's head sits at about x 22.0, y 14.6 on the site. At compass 330° his gaze crosses the driver's seat and lands on room 2's frontage at about x 20.0, some 6 m west of her door. Her B09 lean to the glovebox crosses that line; her B10 and B11 routes do not.
- Left-hand drive is the director's ruling. The casting car-side blocker is closed, so K01 and K02 can lock (a revision note moved out of LOC-01).
- Jean's clothes are #24262B and his shoes #2A2624 (style bible values) in every palette and state. His collar on the bone headrest measures 9.4:1. Revision note moved out of LOC-01's product placement: the version before last gave 6.8:1 in error, and #111111 would give 11.8:1. The shoes on the mat recheck at 12.9:1, which was written as 12.8:1 before (a rounding difference).
- Each prop is listed once, where it first appears in story time. PR-04, PR-05 and PR-56 move to LOC-05, since all are first seen through the bar window in SC04, and LOC-01, LOC-08 and LOC-10 cross-reference them. PR-41 was not named in the note but moves under the same rule. LOC-08 and LOC-10 each still keep more than two props of their own.
- PR-04 follows the style bible. From SC04 to SC14 it hangs by its left arm from the placket on a clear thread tack. In SC15 it lies folded on his folded shirt, and from SC17 it is on his face. PR-05 has no outside pocket, and the old line banning anything from the placket has been removed from PR-56.
- 'Inside left pocket' is used in PR-03 S2/S6, PR-30 S2 and PR-62 S3. PR-06 carries the folded bone jacket, with PR-03 inside it, from the end of SC17 through V01-B11 and SC18. It is packed off screen in LOC-11, so the white apartment stays bare.
- PR-30's end state follows the final script: in SC17 the flat straw goes into the trash on top of the pink cloth, and she pockets only the tin. It does not follow the review note that keeps the straw in the jacket, and that conflict is raised as a blocker.
- LOC-04 follows the film director's framing numbers: the bags inside the latch-side jamb (x 190-245, y 10-50) below the held frame, the jugs in the north-east corner in ranks of 5 and 4, and the floor wear at y 380-480. PR-02 S4 now has her drop the gun into PR-06 at the jamb as she takes PR-28 out, below the held frame. This replaces the package review's tabletop placement, because SC18's table holds the lantern, the oranges and the printer and no gun is written there. The request for a framing check on the gun is withdrawn. If the director wants the gun seen in SC18, it goes to the writer first as an addition.
- SC17's opening insert has its own location, LOC-11, because the script's SC17 heading names it (a revision note moved out of LOC-07). LOC-07 covers SC10 only.
- Keys: PR-61 key 2 locks the glovebox and the trunk. That way the gun, the prints, the report and the next man's prints are never left unlocked overnight in a soft-top on a city street, which follows the script's own SC04 reasoning. Key 2 turns at the glovebox knob as she gets out in SC04, inside the scripted exit. It unlocks both off screen in LOC-11 before the insert, so V01-B09 and B11 need no key action. The groceries and the printer have sat in the locked trunk since before SC04 (ASSUMED), so she never stops with Jean in the car.
- LOC-02 is re-measured. The crossroads is 3.1 km north of the town, which is 6 min 08 s at about 30 km/h (19 mph) from REC 00:00:04 to 00:06:12. The loop from the stop to the second pass at 00:21:40 takes 15 min 28 s: 8.6 km of gravel at about 40 km/h (25 mph, 12.9 min) plus 1.5 km of blacktop at about 35 km/h (22 mph, 2.6 min).
- PR-10 is corrected. The previous version put the sign about 3 m ahead of Jean's head, where a phone on the dash looking back at him cannot see it, so B02's 'the sign behind his head' was false. The sign now stands set back 9 m from the gravel edge (ASSUMED), about 5 m behind his head at the stop. It has been spun on its post by wind to face about 325°, so its face and 'STOP' meet the lens just above and right of his head. The spun sign is a creative addition for Lucas to approve. Fallback: square to the road, so the lens sees only its back and it reads by silhouette. PR-58 is now continuity-only, as the reviewer allowed, because no planned view sees the road ahead of the car.
- Dates: the dash cam reads '10/23/2026 01:47:12' (a Friday, just after the Thursday last call), and the fill slip reads '09/18/2026', exactly five weeks earlier. Both are ASSUMED, chosen to avoid dates with real-world associations, and the writer can change them.
- PR-50 has one hinged outer lid so the fill slip can be 'taped inside its lid'. PR-51 sits on the middle shelf so she can set the organizer 'back square beside' it. The meds appear only in SC17, and the cabinet stays shut in SC11 and SC15.
- PR-47 gains S4 for SC15 (seat still up, the haze dried flat to #EAD0CA) and S5 for SC17 (rim wiped, seat and lid down). S5 is not in the script and goes to the writer. Until it is written, SC17's medicine-cabinet views keep PR-47 out of frame and S4 carries.
- SC09 seating follows the script: Venus directly behind the driver (y 100), Jean by the right rear door. The dash cam sits on the driver's side of the rearview mirror (y 110), so it holds Jean's face and the back of her head. The blocking is the director's.
- The track starts in SC12 from the bedroom speaker while Jean is in the bathroom, and it is heard through the door in SC11. The SC16 roll-over and the fitted corner are keyed into PR-52.
- The flip-camera icon is a camera-body outline with two circular arrows inside, drawn from scratch. It is at least 3% of V01's 16:9 frame width and at least 6% of V02's 9:16 width, and it is in the A10 clearance package.
- The set owns the dust, tracked under PR-57: the dash, Jean's collar (agreed with the stylist) and his eyes (handed to makeup).
- Legible text, written verbatim for compositing: the phone timers, 'STOP', 'MOTEL', 'OFFICE', 'NO CHARGES FILED', the dash-cam '10/23/2026 01:47:12' and the fill slip's '09/18/2026'. Nothing else is legible.
- Surfaces that must stay blank, because generators add fake logos: the car's grille, fenders, wheels, gauges, plate and keys; the storefronts; the bottle labels, fridge, chalkboard, neon and coasters; the rideshare's badges and stickers; the dash-cam body; the mailboxes and door; the speaker, ice bucket and step trash can; both phones' bodies and PR-35's screen; the organizer, vial and toilet paper; the cans, jugs and printer; both bags and the sunglasses' arms; the gas pump and courthouse in the prints; the roller door and lift; and the T-shirt (stylist).
- There is no product (route R2 stretch). Each product_placement names the device doing the job, its contrast and its clear zone instead, and the taste note 'the product is a tool, then a hero' does not apply.
- Taste notes: each location names its one real detail. Two are broken on purpose: the white apartment has no dish rack or charging cable because its tidiness is the horror, and ref 06's patterned bedding becomes white to hold the color theme. V01-B13 and S02 hold more than about five props because the script names them, and LOC-04 gives the lock order.
- References 01-11 were not inspected (no reference reader in this run) and are used only as the brief describes them. No character or set is modeled on a real person or a recognizable place, and every case is fictional.
- Requests to other departments only: the stylist's light-wash denim for the pistol grip; the tin's mirror path; the lantern as a practical with all-round output; the dust hand-offs to the stylist and makeup; the locked car section sent to the cinematographer; SC18's phone placement (with the phone at about x 175, y 370, his face sits about 65° off the lens, a deep three-quarter); the LOC-11 seat read against the lift pads; and PR-47 kept out of SC17 frames until S5 is written.
- Blocker (Cinematographer and generation planner (with the stylist and casting)): P2 moves from 300° to car-frame 240° (compass about 330° heading north; about 150° at the SC18 table). The documents built on 300° are now out of step: the camera plan (SH15-SH17, 'compass 90° to about 30°'), the pose references in the style bible and generation plan (K01, K02, K08), and the casting bible's car-side notes. The camera plan's V01-B02 frame also needs PR-10's new position, about 5 m behind Jean's head and facing the lens. → Re-key SH15-SH17 to 'compass 90° to about 330°' and every P2 reference to car-frame 240°, head only, torso square. Send the reviewers this check: from Jean's head (x 108, y 100), the line to the phone over the wrist arc (x 20-30, y 45-65) runs 238-241°, and their 237° sits inside that spread. Production design has already re-keyed LOC-01, LOC-02, LOC-03 and LOC-04.
- Blocker (Creative director and commissioner (with the stylist, who owns the jacket)): PR-30's fate in SC17. The review note puts the straw, with the tin, in the folded jacket in PR-06 through V01 and SC18. The final script says she drops the flat straw into the trash on top of the pink cloth and 'pockets only' the tin. The script's reason is that she keeps nothing of him except the SC18 photograph. → Built to the script: in PR-30 S5 the straw goes into PR-60, and the jacket in PR-06 carries PR-03 alone. If the reviewers want the straw kept, the writer has to change SC17 first. Nothing else would need to move.
- Blocker (Writer (copywriter)): SC17 does not script the white-apartment bathroom being put right: Jean's raised seat and the dried pink haze on the rim (PR-47 S4) would survive her clean-up. That undercuts 'the horror is how tidy it is', and generators will pick either state at random. → Add half a line to SC17's bathroom action, for example: 'She wipes the rim and lowers the seat and lid.' PR-47 S5 is built and waiting. If the line is not added, SC17's medicine-cabinet views keep the toilet out of frame and S4 carries.
- Blocker (Producer (A10)): Clearance (A10) is pending on the original phone UI, including the flip-camera icon (a camera body with two circular arrows inside), on the 'Naked and Famous' cocktail name, and on the risk that the 1950s roadster silhouette reads as a real make's trade dress. → Add the icon to the A10 clearance package with the REC dot and timer. Everything is built unbranded, with a generic grille and filled emblem holes. If the UI reads as a real OS, simplify it to the REC dot and timer and play the hesitation as the front-camera flip, as the route allows. Nothing is claimed as cleared.
- Blocker (Producer): V02's muted read depends on the viewer knowing what her thumb refuses to do (route success criterion 5), and the current muted test does not check it. → Add the question 'What was her thumb about to do?' to the V02 muted test. A pass is 'turn the camera on herself' or similar. If it fails, production design enlarges the icon past 6% of the frame width before any change to the edit.

### Director's treatment

- Head model, locked: P1 is car-frame 0° (face to his door, compass 90°), with PR-04's right arm trapped between his temple and the pad. P2 is car-frame 240° (compass 330°), a 120° turn of the head alone on the neck through dead ahead, torso square, per world bible LOC-01. At the SC18 table the same set is 30° to his left, gaze about 150°. The head-alone rule is written in SC01 and applies to every dead beat, with the SC02 and SC16 wording: his head never moves relative to his shoulders except under her hand. The script logs it as a stylization, not physiology, and no medical cause is stated.
- V01-B07 restaged per the note: her right hand takes the near (left) arm at the hinge and draws the frame forward along the pad toward the windshield (1.6 s), trimming the drop to jaw height to 0.6 s, so plate 41.6-45.0 still sums to 3.4 s. The 45.0 cut on action now steps from a medium two-shot to a close shot of his head, because the axis moves only about 13°. Shot size is my call; the lens is the DP's.
- Camera-plan shot numbers are read as V01's plate order: SH01 B01; SH02-03 B02; SH04-06 B03; SH07-08 B04; SH09-10 B05; SH11-13 B06; SH14-15 B07; SH16-17 B08; SH18 B09; SH19 B10; SH20-21 B11; SH22-24 B12; SH25 B13-14. I have not seen the camera plan itself. Each ruling carries its own acceptance test, and those tests stand if the numbering differs.
- Rulings on camera-plan blocker 7, unchanged: SH02 sign cheat APPROVED; SH10 road-bump join APPROVED; SH18 vertical jolt APPROVED as spring rock only; SH20 on the axis APPROVED; SH22 at the left third APPROVED; SH23's head above frame RETURNED (the head lift must read from 76.9 to 77.8); SH24 at 325° APPROVED; SC03 hard cut at 82.0 APPROVED; no push in V02-B04 APPROVED as a static punch-in; V02-SH07 thumb entering on f0 APPROVED.
- V01-B13 door: note (d) asks for the drag to be heard before the 82.0 cut. B12 holds 8.5 s of on-screen action in 10 s, with the chair's 2.7 s and the head lift both on the KEEP list, and the door is about 4.2 m from the chair. So the earliest she can drag it shut from outside is 84.2. I stage her steps at 82.0-84.2 and the drag at 84.2-85.2, behind camera, with nothing moving in frame; from 85.2 only the wind pushes at a shut door. The sound-line change is filed with the writer.
- SC10-SC12 re-timed per the note. In SC10 the jacket is hung and PR-01, PR-03 and PR-30 come out of it on screen; the latch clicks at 12.3, inside her 0.8 s look. SC12 0.0 = SC11 33.0, which gives her a 33.0 s off-screen window: tin to PR-42 by 4.0, straw to the kitchen run by 8.5, phone to PR-53 by 16.5, and the change into the T-shirt by 33.0. That assumes the T-shirt is laid out on her bed before SC10 (ASSUMED). The sync points are: tap running at SC11 32.5-34.0; track start at SC11 33.3 = SC12 0.3; ice at 40.5 = 7.5; door at 55.5 = 22.5. The bedroom-to-kitchen walk is 6.5 s. SC11 fills its 60 s slot with a 3.5 s end hold, and SC12's dialogue fills 120 s.
- SC11's extra time before the tap is a toilet beat (22.0 s, back to the lens, sound only, with one cut inside to the shut door) and not a longer vanity beat. The script says Jean is 'in a hurry to get back out to her', and 25 s of preening would contradict that. Using the toilet after seeing the drops also plays his indifference and makes his wipe read as routine. No scripted action changes; the one flush now follows the ice.
- Trance-soul loop: 9.6 s, four bars at 100 BPM, with the hole in bar 4 (SND-D01-001). It starts at SC12 0.3, so SC12's downbeats fall at 0.3, 9.9 and 19.5, and the 19.5 downbeat is SC12's trigger. The editor re-phases it at the SC13-SC14 elision and the SC14-SC15 time cut. In SC15 a downbeat lands on the 150.0 cut; SC16 opens on a downbeat, 'J. Stay for breakfast.' sits in the 45.6-48.0 hole, and the switch-off lands on the 67.2 downbeat. The slots hold at 150 s and 90 s.
- SC08: Venus shoots Jean on PR-35's own front camera by turning the phone round, screen to him, and uses its side button as the shutter, so her thumb never touches his flip icon and SC18 stays the first press. PD confirms that PR-35's case exposes a side button.
- Left-index burn: KEPT and locked (a 1 cm glossy oval on the back of the left index finger, makeup on the hand double, with a paint-in fallback), so K03-HND-VENUS can lock and the hand double can be cast.
- 35mm outside the phone, phone compression and UI inside it; the texture switches at the frame open and close. Stock, grain, lenses and grade belong to the camera department. Every stillness test checks the content after degrain, then live grain and gate weave go back over the whole frame.
- The printer whir is taken as 35.0 s from the practical unit, starting 1.0 s after her thumb taps print at 48.7-49.2. SC18's final wide absorbs any difference.
- SC04-SC14 timings cover the key physical actions, with dialogue filling each slot. SC11 and SC15-SC18 are staged second by second to their full slots (60, 150, 90, 150 and 120 s).
- SC17: Venus's VENUS_L03 clothes are in the bedroom, off screen (ASSUMED). Her change and the phone pickup happen under the 112.0-117.0 cover cut, which is an elision: Jean's stillness hides the minutes.
- D01 scenes SC04-SC18 are staged for a live-action shoot. The 5 s rule and the no-hand-off rule apply to generated V01, V02 and the stills; the hand-offs in SC08 and SC12 are practical only.
- Venus holds the phone in her right hand and drives with her left. Wind is relative, nose to tail, in the moving car and from the south at the motel.
- There is no product, so claim_demo_needs_clearance doesn't arise. The verified story facts are staged exactly, each with an acceptance test.
- Taste notes broken on purpose: S01 shows her laughing face, because a poster needs its lead; the B04 and B08 fallbacks show part of her face for under a second; LOC-08 has no dish rack or cable, because the tidiness is the horror; the trance-soul track enters mid-pilot as the script decided. All other notes are followed.
- US vocabulary and spelling throughout (trunk, curb, tire, pants, trash, organizer, centered, color, favor), matching the script decision and the world bible.
- No reference image was inspected; refs 05 and 06 are used only as the brief describes them.
- Blocker (casting director): The casting bible gives the V01-B07 head turn as 'about 60°'. The world bible's P2 (car-frame 240°) and this treatment need about 120°: from 90° right, through dead ahead, to 30° left, head only, torso square. → Change JEAN's performance_style to 'her two fingers turn his head alone on the neck about 120°, from the door, through dead ahead, to 30° toward the driver's seat'. Add: P1 must read as a head resting on the pad, not a wrenched neck (fallback: ease P1 to about 80° right).
- Blocker (production designer): SC12's scripted pour has no bottle prop; PR-43 is only described as 'clear tequila'. → Add a plain, unlabeled clear glass bottle at the kitchen run beside PR-59, with no text, taken away off screen in SC17. Until then the pour is elided on a cut at SC12 9.0.
- Blocker (production designer): The world bible no longer matches the re-timed staging. PR-01, PR-03 and PR-30 now leave the jacket on screen in SC10, not off screen in SC11. PR-30 has no state for the kitchen run. PR-15 has no state for being dragged shut from outside in V01-B13. PR-35 has no side button listed for the SC08 shutter. VENUS_L02's T-shirt needs a laid-out position for the off-screen change. → Update LOC-08's description and PR-62 S2 to say PR-01, PR-03 and PR-30 come out of the jacket on screen in SC10. Add PR-30 S2b: 'SC11, off screen: laid on the kitchen run beside PR-59; SC12: stood in her ice from the counter.' Add PR-15 S3: 'V01 84.2-85.2, off screen: dragged shut from outside; shut in SC18.' Add to PR-35: a plain side button, exposed through the case. Add to LOC-10: VENUS_L02's T-shirt laid flat on her side of PR-52 before SC10 (ASSUMED).
- Blocker (writer): V01-B13's scripted sound is 'Only the wind outside, pushing at the door'. For the door to be shut and the 250 top-pinned prints to hold still, Venus has to drag PR-15 shut as she leaves. That can't be heard before the 82.0 cut without cutting the chair's 2.7 s or the SH23 head lift, both of which are on the KEEP list. → Amend V01-B13's sound to: 'Her steps crossing the boards and the swollen door dragged shut behind her, then only the wind outside, pushing at the door.' It plays behind camera at 82.0-85.2, with nothing moving in frame. If that is refused, the alternative is to leave the door open and let finishing lock the prints against the draft.
- Blocker (producer): The practical gun beats (SC04, SC18, and SC02 if a practical plate is needed) need a non-firing replica and an armorer. Neither is booked. → Book a non-firing replica and an armorer who checks it before each take and holds it between takes. Status stays pending until confirmed.
- Blocker (cinematographer): V02-B04 to B06 need about 7 s on Jean's eyes, but V01-B08's phone section runs 4.0 s. B04 is now a static punch-in, so the plate has to hold detail at the punched size. → Generate the B08 eyes plate with handles (two plates of up to 5 s joined on a laugh jolt) at 4K equivalent, and shoot the thumb composite plate long.
- Blocker (writer): SC11 carries plot (A13, the previous man's drops) but has no honest link to the route idea; nothing on screen ties the drops to a photograph. It is staged as written. → Either accept SC11 as a plot scene while SC18 carries the idea, or add a 2 s beat before he lifts the seat: Jean takes a mirror selfie in PR-49 with PR-35, one more photo of him alone in her home.

### Looks

- Date and season follow PR-36's timestamp (10/23/2026 01:47, itself marked ASSUMED): the bar night is Thursday 22 October 2026 and the drive is Friday 23 October, US Southwest. Assumed temperatures: city at night about 19 °C, apartment 21 °C, desert 27 °C falling to 17 °C at dusk, motel at night about 15 °C.
- PR-04 ruling, quoted. It was issued by the film director in the package review returned with the previous version of this look sheet; the note carries no date. Target: 'JEAN_L01 accessories (PR-04 hooked in the placket) and its SC04-SC15 continuity lock'. Text: 'Ruling: the sunglasses stay in the duffel's outside pocket until SC17, which is where the world bible, script SC17 and treatment SC17 already put them. Remove the placket hook, the thread tack, the SC15 "folded on top of his shirt" state and the lock line.' The director cites 'World bible PR-04 S0 and PR-56 ("Nothing hangs from the shirt placket in SC04-SC15"); treatment SC17 ("PR-04 out of its outside pocket"); style bible blocker 1'. The world bible in the package I was given says the opposite (PR-04 S0: placket hook and thread tack; PR-05: 'No outside pocket'), and the script's SC17 says only 'puts his sunglasses on him'. I follow the director's ruling and log the mismatch as blocker 1.
- Other IDs I use, all quoted from the same film-director package-review note, which cites 'Style bible JEAN_L02 hair and builds; camera plan SH14 and SH19'. K08 is the kit sheet that 'drives every print on the V01/S02 wall'. SH14 and SH19 are the practical plates in the camera plan. CHR-JEAN-DEAD@v1 is the generated dead-Jean reference the double is approved against. The camera plan, the K08 sheet and that reference are not in the package I was given, so I use the IDs as quoted and cannot check them.
- The next man in PR-28 has a proposed look, PR28_MAN_L01, kept out of the looks list: the checker accepts only casting-bible IDs, and he has none (blocker 7). He is the fictional man on the courthouse steps in two daylight prints, matching no real person or case. October 2026, about 22 °C. Closet: one suit, bought off the rack for court, a size loose. Head to toe: a mid-grey two-piece suit, single-breasted, 2 buttons, jacket buttoned at the top button, hip length, trousers with a full break; a white shirt, collar open at the top button, no tie; dark brown lace-ups. Colours: suit in wool worsted 260 g/m², mid-grey #7E7F80 (approx L*53, C*1). That is 38 L* above Jean's #24262B, and at C*1 it sits well off the paneling #7A5A3C (approx L*41, C*24, h 68°). Shirt: cotton poplin 115 g/m², white #EDEBE5 (approx L*93). Shoes: leather, dark brown #4A3528. Plain unmarked fabric throughout. Fit: jacket shoulder seam 1 cm past the shoulder point; sleeves to the wrist bone, no shirt cuff showing. Hair: straight, mid-brown #5A4535 approx (pending casting), 3 cm on top, a hard side part, combed flat to the skull; clean-shaven. Skin depth and undertone must come from casting. Locks: [each PR-28 print] the grey suit buttoned over a white open collar, no tie: grey and white are the only two blocks, with nothing black above the shoes. [each PR-28 print] Hair flat to the skull with the side part on the same side in both prints; no beard, no sunglasses.
- Third-night wear on SH-1 and TR-1 (soft lifted collar tips, crumples at both inner elbows, sit creases across the lap) holds from SC04 to SC15. The creases carry into JEAN_L02 because the clothes are never pressed. What she adds is visibly different: sharp fold creases, the fastened top button and flat collar points. The K08-A prints show SH-1 without the third-night creases, because they are earlier nights.
- The K08 dates (June to October 2026) and shares (K08-A about 100 prints, K08-B about 100, K08-C about 50) are assumed. They fill PR-08's 'going back months' and PR-20's 'about half night, half day' within the ~250 count the world bible assumes. The wall's grid order and which print sits where belong to production design.
- Jean's black: the world bible carries #24262B for PR-56 and #2A2624 for PR-41. The route's #111111 stays the graded read for the colourist. All K08 blacks sit at approx L*15-17 in the same blue cast.
- Pocket carries: a matched pair of 19 x 15 cm back pockets, with a drill sling in the right one whose fold sits 6 cm below the mouth, so 6 cm of grip shows. PR-02's 17 x 12 x 3 cm size and 600 g weight are assumed. The VENUS_L01 chest welt has a 9 cm opening and a 13.5 cm bag and leaves 1.5 cm of glass showing; PR-01 is assumed at about 200 g, carried on stay tapes, lens end down.
- Venus's three hand marks are locked by the director's ruling, as recorded in the casting bible. In every look both sleeves end 12 cm above the wrist bone, and the hand double carries the same marks.
- Jean in SC15-SC16 and Venus in L02 each wear a skin-matched modesty garment that is never seen. The sheet stays at his waist through the SC16 roll, so no piece needs nudity.
- The L02 hem numbers rest on assumed proportions for a 177 cm woman: the high point of the shoulder at about 145 cm and the top of the kneecap at about 51 cm. The fitter holds the standing mark.
- There is no product (R2 stretch), so the hue rule is run against the story's two reds: the REC dot #D7261E (approx L*47, C*82, h 37°) and the dried drops #6E1C18 (approx L*24, C*43, h 34°). Every garment in frame with them is either under C* 15 or far off in hue. Skin and hair are excluded, because skin is never altered.
- One garment per look is older than the story, each with a rebuild method: Venus's pistol-worn back pocket and her L02 tee, Jean's belt, the bartender's apron, and the driver's sun-faded shirt (taste note: one real detail per scene).
- Taste notes followed: hands before faces (every acting hand is bare, with cuffs at mid-forearm or above) and restraint over explanation (no symbolic jewellery; her whites and his black carry the verified colour theme). One craft rule is broken on purpose: VENUS_L02 stays inside the white room's palette so that Jean is the only dark mass.
- I did not inspect any reference image, because no reference_reader is bound. The references are used only as the brief describes them, and no character is modelled on a face in them.
- Blocker (Production designer (world bible); film director (K08 kit sheet and the lost plant)): The world bible I was given contradicts the film director's PR-04 ruling (quoted in the assumptions). It reads as follows. PR-04 S0, SC04-SC14: 'hooked by the left arm in Jean's placket over the fastened second button, held with a clear thread tack'. PR-04 S1 and PR-55 S2, SC15: 'folded, lenses up, on top of his folded shirt'. PR-04 S2, SC17: 'taken from that pile'. PR-56 S0-S2 and the LOC-05 description: 'PR-04 hooked in his placket'. PR-05: 'No outside pocket'. The director reports that the placket version has also reached the K08 kit sheet that drives every Jean print on the V01/S02 wall. Under the ruling one plant is lost. The world bible had the glasses on Jean alive, seen through the bar window in SC04, which tied PR-04 to him before V01. In story order they now first appear when she takes them out of his duffel in SC17. That loss is the director's to rule on. I have not restored it: there are no sunglasses in any K08 print. → Production design: set PR-04 S0 (SC04-SC16) to PR-05's outside pocket; S1 (SC17) taken out of that pocket and put on his face before she zips the duffel. Give PR-05 its outside pocket; size and side are production design's call. Delete PR-04 from PR-55 S2, PR-56 S0-S2 and LOC-05, and add to PR-56: 'Nothing hangs from the shirt placket in SC04-SC15.' Film director: regenerate K08 from the JEAN_L01 rail (K08-A/B/C), with the placket bare in every print. Also rule whether the lost plant needs a replacement. One option, not applied here: PR-04 on his face in some K08-B day prints.
- Blocker (Casting director and film director): Jean's double for the SH14 and SH19 plates has not been cast, so wardrobe can't fit SH-2 at C3 on him, and hair can't decide between cutting his own 4A hair and building the custom topper, which needs build time. → Casting supplies the double and books him a fitting. Hair cuts his own 4A hair to spec or builds the topper. Wardrobe fits SH-2 at C3 (or cuts him his own SH-2 from the same cloth). Wardrobe and hair sign off side by side with CHR-JEAN-DEAD@v1.
- Blocker (Production designer): Two pocket carries still need the prop owner's sign-off. (a) PR-02 rides slide down on a drill sling 6 cm inside the right back pocket, grip up at the side-seam end, muzzle toward the centre back, 6 cm of grip showing; its 17 x 12 x 3 cm size and about 600 g weight are assumed. (b) PR-01 rides lens end down in the jacket's chest welt, but PR-01 S2 says only 'screen-in'. → Confirm PR-02's orientation and weight in S1 and S3, adding 'slide down on the pocket sling, grip at the side-seam end'. Add 'lens end down' to PR-01 S2. If the prop comes in heavier or taller, wardrobe moves the sling fold so that 6 cm of grip still shows.
- Blocker (Film director): Two blocking points follow from the looks and aren't in the script yet. (a) SC17: Venus changes off screen from the tee (L02) into the pressed shirt (L03), after the straw goes into PR-60 and before she closes the tin, washing off the kohl and putting her hair up. (b) SC18: wardrobe needs the elastics out and her hair down while she stands, and PR-01 out of the right front pocket before she sits. → Cut away for the SC17 change (the finished-room frame can cover it) and bring her back in L03 to close the tin. Set the SC18 order and tell wardrobe.
- Blocker (Lucas (creative lead) via producer, with production designer): Lucas's 'oversized Raptors t shirt' has lost its team. The L02 tee reads as an athletic shirt by its raglan cut and V-notch, but no team, city or colour can be shown until A10 clears the real shirt or a fictional team is designed and cleared. → Lucas chooses: accept the unmarked athletic tee, or have production design create a fictional team for the producer to clear under A10. If team colours come in, wardrobe re-runs the white-room rule break, because a saturated or dark team colour would put a second dark mass beside Jean.
- Blocker (Lucas (creative lead) via casting director): Lucas still has to confirm heritage for VENUS and JEAN (carried over from the casting bible). - Blocker (Casting director, with production designer (PR-28 owner)): The next man in PR-28's two courthouse-steps prints has no casting-bible entry. Wardrobe then moves PR28_MAN_L01 into the looks list and restates skin and hair from that entry.
